**6.28.44**  For by that very practice of his former life he is carried onward, even against his will; and merely seeking to know yoga, he passes beyond the Brahman of the word.

**6.28.45**  But the yogin who strives with effort, his stains thoroughly purified, perfected through many births, then attains the supreme goal.

**6.28.46**  The yogin is greater than the ascetics, and held to be greater even than the men of knowledge, and the yogin is greater than the men of works; therefore become a yogin, O Arjuna.

**6.28.47**  And of all yogins, he who worships Me full of faith, his inmost self gone forth to Me—him I hold to be the most deeply yoked.

**6.29.1**  The Blessed Lord said: With your mind attached to Me, O Pārtha, practicing yoga and taking refuge in Me, hear how you shall know Me wholly and without doubt.

**6.29.2**  I shall declare to you in full this knowledge together with its realization, knowing which nothing further remains here to be known.

**6.29.3**  Among thousands of men, scarcely one strives for perfection; and even among those who strive and attain it, scarcely one knows Me in truth.

**6.29.4**  Earth, water, fire, air, ether, mind, intellect, and ego-sense — thus is My nature divided eightfold.

**6.29.5**  This is My lower nature; but know, O mighty-armed one, My other and higher nature, which has become the living soul, by which this world is sustained.

**6.29.6**  Understand that all beings have their womb in these two natures. I am the origin of the whole universe, and likewise its dissolution.

**6.29.7**  There is nothing whatever higher than Me, O Dhanaṃjaya. Upon Me all this is strung, like rows of pearls upon a thread.

**6.29.8**  I am the savor in the waters, O son of Kuntī; I am the radiance in the moon and the sun; the sacred syllable Oṃ in all the Vedas, sound in the ether, and manhood in men.

**6.29.9**  I am the pure fragrance in the earth and the brilliance in fire; I am the life in all beings, and the austerity in ascetics.

**6.29.10**  Know Me, O Pārtha, as the eternal seed of all beings. I am the intelligence of the intelligent, the splendor of the splendid.

**6.29.11**  I am the strength of the strong, freed from desire and passion; and in beings I am desire that is not opposed to dharma, O bull of the Bharatas.

**6.29.12**  And whatever states of being there are — of sattva, of rajas, or of tamas — know that they proceed from Me alone; yet I am not in them, but they are in Me.

**6.29.13**  Deluded by these three states composed of the guṇas, all this world fails to recognize Me, who am beyond them and imperishable.

**6.29.14**  For this divine māyā of Mine, composed of the guṇas, is hard to overcome; but those who take refuge in Me alone cross beyond this māyā.

**6.29.15**  The evildoers, the deluded, the basest of men do not take refuge in Me; their knowledge carried off by māyā, they cleave to the demonic nature.

**6.29.16**  Four kinds of virtuous men worship Me, O Arjuna: the afflicted, the seeker of knowledge, the seeker of gain, and the one who knows, O bull of the Bharatas.

**6.29.17**  Of these, the knower, ever steadfast and devoted to the One, excels; for to the knower I am exceedingly dear, and he is dear to Me.

**6.29.18**  Noble indeed are all of these, but the knower I hold to be My very Self; for, his soul made steadfast, he is established in Me alone as the supreme goal.

**6.29.19**  At the end of many births, the man of knowledge takes refuge in Me, realizing, 'Vāsudeva is all.' Such a great soul is very hard to find.

**6.29.20**  Those whose knowledge is carried off by this or that desire resort to other deities, observing this or that rite, constrained by their own nature.

**6.29.21**  Whatever form any devotee desires to worship with faith, that same unwavering faith I bestow upon him.

**6.29.22**  Endowed with that faith, he seeks to propitiate that form, and from it he obtains his desires — desires ordained by Me alone.

**6.29.23**  But the fruit gained by these men of little understanding is perishable. The worshippers of the gods go to the gods; My devotees come to Me.

**6.29.24**  The unintelligent think of Me, the unmanifest, as having come into manifestation, not knowing My higher nature, imperishable and supreme.

**6.29.25**  I am not revealed to all, veiled as I am by My yoga-māyā. This deluded world does not recognize Me, the unborn and imperishable.

**6.29.26**  I know the beings that are past, that are present, and that are yet to come, O Arjuna; but Me no one knows.

**6.29.27**  By the delusion of the pairs of opposites, sprung from desire and aversion, O Bhārata, all beings fall into bewilderment at their birth, O scorcher of foes.

**6.29.28**  But those men of virtuous deeds in whom sin has come to an end — they, freed from the delusion of the pairs of opposites, worship Me, firm in their vows.

**6.29.29**  Those who take refuge in Me and strive for release from old age and death — they come to know that Brahman in its fullness, the supreme Self, and action in its entirety.

**6.29.30**  Those who know Me together with the Adhibhūta, the Adhidaiva, and the Adhiyajña — they, their minds made steadfast, know Me even at the hour of departure.

**6.30.1**  Arjuna said: What is that Brahman? What is the Adhyātma, the supreme Self? What is action, O best of beings? What is declared to be the Adhibhūta, and what is called the Adhidaiva?

**6.30.2**  Who and how is the Adhiyajña here in this body, O slayer of Madhu? And how, at the hour of departure, are You to be known by those of disciplined self?

**6.30.3**  The Blessed Lord said: The imperishable, the supreme, is Brahman; one's own essential nature is called the Adhyātma; and the creative force that brings about the origin of the states of beings is named action (karman).

**6.30.4**  The Adhibhūta is the perishable existence; the Puruṣa is the Adhidaiva; and the Adhiyajña am I Myself, here within this body, O best of the embodied.

**6.30.5**  And whoever, at the hour of his end, departs leaving the body while remembering Me alone, attains to My being; of this there is no doubt.

**6.30.6**  Whatever state of being one remembers when at the last he gives up the body, to that very state he goes, O son of Kuntī, ever shaped by the thought of it.

**6.30.7**  Therefore at all times remember Me, and fight. With mind and intellect fixed on Me, you shall surely come to Me alone.

**6.30.8**  With a mind made steadfast by the yoga of practice, not straying to anything else, meditating upon Him, one attains the supreme, the divine Puruṣa, O Pārtha.

**6.30.9**  He who meditates upon the Seer, the ancient, the ruler, subtler than the subtlest, the sustainer of all, whose form is beyond thought, sun-hued, beyond the darkness —

**6.30.10**  at the hour of departure, with unwavering mind, endowed with devotion and with the power of yoga, fixing the breath rightly between the brows — he attains that supreme, divine Puruṣa.

**6.30.11**  That which the knowers of the Veda call the imperishable, which the passion-free ascetics enter, desiring which men practice the discipline of brahmacarya — that state I shall declare to you in brief.

**6.30.12**  Controlling all the gates of the body, confining the mind within the heart, fixing one's own breath in the head, established in the steadfastness of yoga,

**6.30.13**  uttering the single syllable Oṃ, which is Brahman, and remembering Me — he who thus departs, giving up the body, goes to the supreme goal.

**6.30.14**  To him whose mind dwells on no other, who remembers Me constantly and without ceasing — to that ever-steadfast yogin, O Pārtha, I am easy to attain.

**6.30.15**  Having come to Me, the great souls who have reached the highest perfection do not return to rebirth, that impermanent abode of sorrow.

**6.30.16**  All the worlds, up to the realm of Brahmā, are subject to return, O Arjuna; but having come to Me, O son of Kuntī, there is no rebirth.

**6.30.17**  Those who know that a day of Brahmā lasts a thousand ages, and that his night too ends only after a thousand ages — they are the men who know day and night.

**6.30.18**  At the coming of the day, all manifestations spring forth from the unmanifest; at the coming of the night they dissolve back into that same source called the unmanifest.

**6.30.19**  This same multitude of beings, coming into existence again and again, dissolves helplessly at the coming of the night, O Pārtha, and comes forth again at the coming of the day.

**6.30.20**  But higher than that unmanifest is another, an eternal Unmanifest, which, when all beings perish, does not itself perish.

**6.30.21**  That Unmanifest is called the Imperishable; they declare it the supreme goal, attaining which men do not return. That is My supreme abode.

**6.30.22**  That supreme Puruṣa, O Pārtha, within whom all beings dwell and by whom all this is pervaded, is to be attained by undivided devotion.

**6.30.23**  Now I shall tell you, O bull of the Bharatas, of the time at which yogins, having departed, go to return no more, and of that at which they go to return again.

**6.30.24**  Fire, light, the day, the bright fortnight, the six months of the sun's northern course — departing then, men who know Brahman go to Brahman.

**6.30.25**  Smoke, the night, likewise the dark fortnight, the six months of the sun's southern course — departing then, the yogin attains the lunar light and returns.

**6.30.26**  These two paths of the world, the bright and the dark, are held to be eternal. By the one a man goes never to return; by the other he returns again.

**6.30.27**  Knowing these two paths, O Pārtha, no yogin is ever deluded. Therefore at all times be steadfast in yoga, O Arjuna.

**6.30.28**  Whatever fruit of merit is laid down for the Vedas, for sacrifices, for austerities, and for gifts — the yogin, knowing all this, transcends it all and attains the supreme, primal abode.

**6.31.1**  The Blessed Lord said: To you, who do not cavil, I shall now declare this most secret knowledge together with its realization, knowing which you shall be freed from evil.

**6.31.2**  This is the king of sciences, the king of secrets, the supreme purifier, directly intelligible, in accord with dharma, easy to practice, and imperishable.

**6.31.3**  Men without faith in this dharma, O scorcher of foes, fail to attain Me and return upon the path of death and the round of rebirth.

**6.31.4**  By Me, in My unmanifest form, all this universe is pervaded. All beings abide in Me, but I do not abide in them.

**6.31.5**  And yet the beings do not abide in Me — behold My sovereign yoga! Sustaining all beings, yet not dwelling in them, My Self is the source of beings.

**6.31.6**  As the mighty wind, ranging everywhere, abides forever in the ether, so understand that all beings abide in Me.

**6.31.7**  All beings, O son of Kuntī, return into My nature at the end of a kalpa; at the beginning of the next kalpa I send them forth again.

**6.31.8**  Resting upon My own nature, I send forth again and again this whole multitude of beings, helpless under the sway of that nature.

**6.31.9**  Nor do these actions bind Me, O Dhanaṃjaya, for I remain seated as one indifferent, unattached to those actions.

**6.31.10**  Under My oversight, nature brings forth all that moves and does not move; by this cause, O son of Kuntī, the world revolves.

**6.31.11**  The deluded despise Me, dwelling as I do in a human form, not knowing My higher nature as the great Lord of all beings.

**6.31.12**  Vain in their hopes, vain in their deeds, vain in their knowledge, and bereft of discernment, they cleave to a deluding nature, demonic and fiendish.

**6.31.13**  But the great souls, O Pārtha, who have taken refuge in the divine nature, worship Me with minds undistracted, knowing Me as the imperishable source of all beings.

**6.31.14**  Ever glorifying Me, striving, firm in their vows, and bowing before Me with devotion, ever steadfast they worship Me.

**6.31.15**  Others too, sacrificing through the sacrifice of knowledge, worship Me — as the One, as the manifold, as variously present, facing in every direction.

**6.31.16**  I am the rite, I am the sacrifice, I am the offering to the ancestors, I am the healing herb, I am the sacred chant, I am the clarified butter, I am the fire, and I am the oblation.

**6.31.17**  I am the father of this world, the mother, the sustainer, the grandsire; I am the object of knowledge, the purifier, the syllable Oṃ, and the Ṛk, the Sāman, and the Yajus as well.

**6.31.18**  I am the goal, the upholder, the lord, the witness, the abode, the refuge, the friend; the origin, the dissolution, the foundation, the resting-place, and the imperishable seed.

**6.31.19**  I give heat; I withhold the rain and send it forth. I am immortality and also death; I am being and non-being, O Arjuna.

**6.31.20**  The knowers of the three Vedas, the soma-drinkers, purified of sin, worshipping Me with sacrifices, pray for passage to heaven; reaching the holy world of Indra, lord of the gods, they enjoy in heaven the divine pleasures of the gods.

**6.31.21**  Having enjoyed that vast heavenly world, when their merit is exhausted they enter the world of mortals. Thus, following the dharma of the three Vedas and craving objects of desire, they obtain only the coming and the going.

**6.31.22**  But to those men who, thinking on no other, worship Me — to them, ever steadfast, I bring attainment of what they lack and preservation of what they have.

**6.31.23**  Even those devotees of other gods who sacrifice endowed with faith — they too sacrifice to Me alone, O son of Kuntī, though not according to the prescribed rite.

**6.31.24**  For I am the enjoyer and the lord of all sacrifices; but men do not truly know me, and therefore they fall away.

**6.31.25**  Those vowed to the gods go to the gods; those vowed to the ancestors go to the ancestors; the worshippers of spirits go to the spirits; but those who sacrifice to me come to me.

**6.31.26**  Whoever offers me with devotion a leaf, a flower, a fruit, or water—that offering, presented with devotion by the pure of heart, I accept and partake of.

**6.31.27**  Whatever you do, whatever you eat, whatever you offer in sacrifice, whatever you give, whatever austerity you perform—do that, O son of Kuntī, as an offering to me.

**6.31.28**  Thus you shall be freed from the bonds of action that bear fruits good and ill; with your self yoked to the discipline of renunciation, liberated, you shall come to me.

**6.31.29**  I am the same toward all beings; to me none is hateful and none is dear. But those who worship me with devotion are in me, and I too am in them.

**6.31.30**  Even if a man of the most evil conduct worships me with undivided devotion, he is to be reckoned righteous, for he has rightly resolved.

**6.31.31**  Swiftly he becomes righteous of soul and attains lasting peace. Know this for certain, O son of Kuntī: my devotee never perishes.

**6.31.32**  For taking refuge in me, O Pārtha, even those of sinful birth—women, vaiśyas, and śūdras alike—they too attain the supreme goal.

**6.31.33**  How much more, then, the holy brāhmaṇas and devout royal sages! Having come into this impermanent and joyless world, worship me.

**6.31.34**  Fix your mind on me, be devoted to me, sacrifice to me, bow down to me; having thus yoked your self with me as your highest goal, you shall come to me alone.

**6.32.1**  The Blessed Lord said: Once more, O mighty-armed, hear my supreme word, which I shall speak to you, who delight in me, out of desire for your good.

**6.32.2**  Neither the hosts of gods nor the great sages know my origin, for I am in every way the source of the gods and the great sages.

**6.32.3**  Whoever knows me as unborn and without beginning, the great lord of the worlds, he, undeluded among mortals, is freed from all sins.

**6.32.4**  Understanding, knowledge, freedom from delusion, patience, truth, self-restraint, tranquility, pleasure, pain, being, non-being, fear, and fearlessness—

**6.32.5**  non-violence, equanimity, contentment, austerity, charity, fame, and infamy—these manifold states of beings arise from me alone.

**6.32.6**  The seven great sages of old, and likewise the four Manus, sharing in my nature, were born of my mind; from them came these creatures of the world.

**6.32.7**  Whoever truly knows this manifest power and the yoga of mine is united with me through unshakable yoga; of this there is no doubt.

**6.32.8**  I am the source of all; from me everything proceeds. Knowing this, the wise, endowed with feeling, worship me.

**6.32.9**  With their thoughts fixed on me, their lives surrendered to me, enlightening one another and ever speaking of me, they are content and they rejoice.

**6.32.10**  To those who are ever steadfast and worship me with love, I grant that yoga of understanding by which they come to me.

**6.32.11**  Out of compassion for them alone, dwelling within their being, I destroy the darkness born of ignorance with the shining lamp of knowledge.

**6.32.12**  Arjuna said: You are the supreme Brahman, the supreme abode, the supreme purifier; the eternal, divine Spirit, the primal god, unborn and all-pervading.

**6.32.13**  So all the seers proclaim you—the divine sage Nārada, and Asita Devala, and Vyāsa; and you yourself declare it to me.

**6.32.14**  All that you tell me I hold to be true, O Keśava; for neither the gods nor the dānavas know your manifestation, O Blessed One.

**6.32.15**  You alone know yourself by yourself, O supreme Spirit, source of beings, lord of beings, god of gods, master of the world.

**6.32.16**  You alone are fit to declare in full your divine self-manifestations, by which manifest powers you stand pervading these worlds.

**6.32.17**  How may I know you, O master of yoga, by meditating on you constantly? In what various forms, O Blessed One, are you to be contemplated by me?

**6.32.18**  Tell me again in detail of your yoga and your manifest power, O Janārdana; for as I listen there is no satiety in me for this nectar.

**6.32.19**  The Blessed Lord said: Come, I shall declare to you my divine self-manifestations—but only the chief of them, O best of the Kurus, for there is no end to my extent.

**6.32.20**  I am the Self, O Guḍākeśa, seated in the heart of all beings; I am the beginning, the middle, and the very end of all beings.

**6.32.21**  Among the Ādityas I am Viṣṇu; among lights, the radiant sun; among the Maruts I am Marīci; among the lunar mansions I am the moon.

**6.32.22**  Among the Vedas I am the Sāmaveda; among the gods I am Vāsava; among the senses I am the mind; in beings I am consciousness.

**6.32.23**  Among the Rudras I am Śaṃkara; among the yakṣas and rākṣasas, the lord of wealth; among the Vasus I am fire; among the peaks I am Meru.

**6.32.24**  Among priests know me to be their chief, Bṛhaspati, O Pārtha; among commanders of armies I am Skanda; among waters I am the ocean.

**6.32.25**  Among the great sages I am Bhṛgu; among utterances I am the single syllable Oṃ; among sacrifices I am the sacrifice of muttered prayer; among the immovable, the Himālaya.

**6.32.26**  Among all trees I am the aśvattha; among the divine sages, Nārada; among the gandharvas, Citraratha; among the perfected ones, the sage Kapila.

**6.32.27**  Among horses know me to be Uccaiḥśravas, born of the nectar; among lordly elephants, Airāvata; and among men, the king.

**6.32.28**  Among weapons I am the thunderbolt; among cows I am Kāmadhuk, the wish-granting cow; I am Kandarpa, the begetter; among serpents I am Vāsuki.

**6.32.29**  Among the nāgas I am Ananta; among the dwellers in the waters I am Varuṇa; among the ancestors I am Aryaman; among those who govern I am Yama.

**6.32.30**  Among the daityas I am Prahlāda; among reckoners I am Time; among beasts I am the king of beasts, the lion; and among birds I am Vainateya, Garuḍa.

**6.32.31**  Among purifiers I am the wind; among bearers of weapons I am Rāma; among fishes I am the makara; among flowing rivers I am the Jāhnavī, the Gaṅgā.

**6.32.32**  Of creations I am the beginning, the end, and the middle as well, O Arjuna; among sciences I am the science of the supreme Self; among those who debate, I am their reasoned argument.

**6.32.33**  Among letters I am the letter A; among compounds I am the copulative dvandva. I alone am imperishable Time; I am the Sustainer facing in every direction.

**6.32.34**  I am all-seizing Death, and the origin of things yet to be; and among feminine powers I am fame, fortune, speech, memory, intelligence, constancy, and patience.

**6.32.35**  Among the Sāman hymns I am the Bṛhatsāman; among meters I am the Gāyatrī; among months I am Mārgaśīrṣa; among seasons I am the flower-bearing spring.

**6.32.36**  Among the deceitful I am the gambling of dice; I am the splendor of the splendid; I am victory, I am resolve; I am the goodness of the good.

**6.32.37**  Among the Vṛṣṇis I am Vāsudeva; among the Pāṇḍavas, Dhanaṃjaya; among sages I am Vyāsa; among seers I am Uśanā the poet.

**6.32.38**  Among those who chastise I am the rod; among those who seek victory I am statecraft; among secret things I am silence; and I am the wisdom of the wise.

**6.32.39**  And whatever is the seed of all beings, that too am I, O Arjuna; there is no being, moving or unmoving, that could exist apart from me.

**6.32.40**  There is no end to my divine manifestations, O scorcher of foes; what I have declared of the extent of my power is but by way of illustration.

**6.32.41**  Whatever being possesses power, beauty, or might, know that each has sprung from a fragment of my splendor.

**6.32.42**  But what need have you, O Arjuna, to know all this in detail? With a single fragment of myself I stand supporting this entire world.

**6.33.1**  Arjuna said: By the supreme and secret word concerning the Self, which out of grace toward me you have spoken, this delusion of mine has been dispelled.

**6.33.2**  For the arising and the passing away of beings I have heard from you in full, O lotus-eyed one, and your imperishable majesty as well.

**6.33.3**  Even as you have declared yourself to be, O supreme Lord, so it is. Yet I long to behold your sovereign form, O supreme Spirit.

**6.33.4**  If you think it possible for me to behold it, O Lord, then, O master of yoga, show me your imperishable Self.

**6.33.5**  The Blessed Lord said: Behold my forms, O Pārtha, by the hundreds and by the thousands—manifold and divine, of many colors and many shapes.

**6.33.6**  Behold the Ādityas, the Vasus, the Rudras, the two Aśvins, and the Maruts; behold, O descendant of Bharata, many marvels never seen before.

**6.33.7**  Here, gathered together in one place within my body, behold now the whole world, with all that moves and is unmoving, O Guḍākeśa, and whatever else you wish to see.

**6.33.8**  But you cannot behold me with this eye of your own; I give you a divine eye—behold my sovereign yoga.

**6.33.9**  Saṃjaya said: Having spoken thus, O king, Hari, the great lord of yoga, then revealed to Pārtha his supreme sovereign form—

**6.33.10**  with many mouths and eyes, with many wondrous aspects, with many divine ornaments, with many divine weapons raised on high;

**6.33.11**  wearing divine garlands and raiment, anointed with divine fragrances—the all-marvelous god, infinite, facing in every direction.

**6.33.12**  If the light of a thousand suns were to blaze forth all at once in the sky, that might resemble the splendor of that great-souled one.

**6.33.13**  There, gathered together in one place yet divided in manifold ways, the son of Pāṇḍu then beheld the whole world within the body of the god of gods.

**6.33.14**  Then Dhanaṃjaya, seized with wonder and his hair standing on end, bowing his head to the god with joined palms, spoke:

**6.33.15**  Arjuna said: I behold within your body, O god, the gods and all the assembled hosts of beings—Brahmā the lord seated upon his lotus throne, and all the seers and the divine serpents.

**6.33.16**  With many arms, bellies, mouths, and eyes I behold you, infinite of form on every side. I see in you no end, no middle, nor any beginning, O lord of all, O form of all.

**6.33.17**  Crowned with a diadem, bearing mace and discus, a mass of radiance blazing on every side—I behold you, hard to gaze upon all around, with the brilliance of blazing fire and sun, immeasurable.

**6.33.18**  You are the supreme Imperishable, the one to be known; you are the highest resting-place of this universe; you are the changeless guardian of the eternal dharma; you are the everlasting Spirit, so I deem you.

**6.33.19**  Without beginning, middle, or end, of infinite power, with countless arms, the moon and sun your eyes—I behold you, your mouth a blazing sacrificial fire, scorching this universe with your own splendor.

**6.33.20**  This space between heaven and earth, and all the quarters, are pervaded by you alone. Seeing this wondrous and terrible form of yours, the three worlds tremble in anguish, O great-souled one.

**6.33.21**  Yonder hosts of gods enter into you; some, afraid, with joined palms extol you. Crying "Hail!" the throngs of great sages and perfected ones praise you with abundant hymns of praise.

**6.33.22**  The Rudras, the Ādityas, the Vasus, and the Sādhyas, the Viśvedevas, the two Aśvins, the Maruts, and the drinkers of vapor, the hosts of gandharvas, yakṣas, asuras, and siddhas—all gaze upon you, and all are amazed.

**6.33.23**  Beholding your mighty form of many mouths and eyes, O mighty-armed, of many arms, thighs, and feet, of many bellies, terrible with many fangs, the worlds tremble in anguish, and so do I.

**6.33.24**  Touching the sky, ablaze, of many hues, with gaping mouths and vast blazing eyes—seeing you thus, my inmost self trembles, and I find neither steadiness nor peace, O Viṣṇu.

**6.33.25**  Seeing your mouths, terrible with fangs and resembling the fire of universal dissolution, I know not the quarters of the sky, and I find no refuge. Be gracious, O lord of gods, O dwelling-place of the world.

**6.33.26**  And yonder, the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, all of them, together with the hosts of kings—Bhīṣma, Droṇa, and that son of the charioteer too—along with our own foremost warriors as well,

**6.33.27**  hastening, they enter your mouths, terrible with fangs and dreadful; some are seen clinging between your teeth, their heads ground to powder.

**6.33.28**  As the many torrents of the rivers race straight toward the sea, so do these heroes of the world of men enter your blazing mouths.

**6.33.29**  As moths in headlong speed plunge into a blazing fire to their destruction, so too do these peoples plunge into your mouths in headlong speed, to their destruction.

**6.33.30**  Devouring on every side, you lick up all the worlds with your flaming mouths; filling the whole universe with radiance, your terrible splendors scorch it, O Viṣṇu.

**6.33.31**  Tell me who you are, you of terrible form. Homage to you, O best of gods; be gracious. I long to know you, the Primal One, for I do not comprehend your working.

**6.33.32**  The Blessed Lord said: I am Time, grown mighty, the destroyer of the worlds, set in motion here to annihilate the worlds. Even without you, all these warriors arrayed in the opposing ranks shall cease to be.

**6.33.33**  Therefore rise up and win glory; conquer your foes and enjoy a flourishing kingdom. By me alone are these already slain; be you merely the instrument, O Savyasācin.

**6.33.34**  Droṇa and Bhīṣma and Jayadratha, and Karṇa, and the other warrior-heroes as well — slay them, already slain by me. Do not falter. Fight! You shall conquer your rivals in battle.

**6.33.35**  Saṃjaya said: Hearing this speech of Keśava, the Diademed One, trembling, his palms joined, made obeisance, and bowing low spoke once more to Kṛṣṇa in a faltering voice, sore afraid.

**6.33.36**  Arjuna said: Rightly, O Hṛṣīkeśa, does the world rejoice and take delight in your glory; the rākṣasas flee in terror to every quarter, and all the hosts of the siddhas bow before you.

**6.33.37**  And why should they not bow to you, O great-souled one, greater even than Brahmā, the first creator? O infinite Lord of gods, abode of the universe, you are the Imperishable, the existent, the non-existent, and what lies beyond both.

**6.33.38**  You are the primal God, the ancient Spirit; you are the supreme resting-place of this universe. You are the knower and the known, the highest abode; by you the universe is pervaded, O you of infinite form.

**6.33.39**  You are Vāyu, Yama, Agni, Varuṇa, the moon, Prajāpati, and the great-grandsire. Homage, homage to you a thousandfold, and again and yet again, homage, homage to you!

**6.33.40**  Homage to you from before and from behind; homage to you on every side, O All. Of infinite valor and boundless might, you encompass all things, and therefore you are all.

**6.33.41**  Whatever I said rashly, thinking you but a friend — 'Hey Kṛṣṇa! Hey Yādava! Hey friend!' — in ignorance of this your majesty, through heedlessness or through affection,

**6.33.42**  and however, for jest's sake, I slighted you at play, in bed, while seated, or at meals, whether alone or before others, O Acyuta — for that I beg your pardon, O immeasurable one.

**6.33.43**  You are the father of the world, of all that moves and is still; you are its object of worship, its venerable teacher. There is none equal to you — how then could there be another greater? — in the three worlds, O you of incomparable power.

**6.33.44**  Therefore, bowing down and prostrating my body, I implore your grace, O Lord, worthy of praise. As a father bears with a son, a friend with a friend, a lover with his beloved, so should you bear with me, O God.

**6.33.45**  I am thrilled to have seen what was never seen before, yet my mind is shaken with fear. Show me, O God, that other form of yours; be gracious, O Lord of gods, abode of the universe.

**6.33.46**  I long to see you just as before, crowned with the diadem, bearing the mace and the discus in your hand. Take on once more that four-armed form, O thousand-armed one, O you whose body is the universe.

**6.33.47**  The Blessed Lord said: By my grace, O Arjuna, and through my own power, this supreme form of mine has been shown to you — full of light, universal, infinite, primal — which none but you has ever beheld.

**6.33.48**  Not by study of the Vedas and sacrifices, not by gifts, not by rites, not by fierce austerities, can I be seen in this form in the world of men by any other than you, O foremost of the Kurus.

**6.33.49**  Let there be no fear in you, no bewilderment, at beholding this dread form of mine. Free from fear, glad of heart, behold once more this very form of mine.

**6.33.50**  Saṃjaya said: Having thus spoken to Arjuna, Vāsudeva revealed again his own form, and the great-souled one, becoming once more gentle of aspect, comforted him in his terror.

**6.33.51**  Arjuna said: Beholding this gentle human form of yours, O Janārdana, I am now composed, my mind restored, returned to my natural state.

**6.33.52**  The Blessed Lord said: This form of mine that you have seen is very hard to behold; even the gods forever long for the sight of this form.

**6.33.53**  Not by the Vedas, not by austerity, not by almsgiving, nor by sacrifice, can I be seen in such a form as you have seen me.

**6.33.54**  But by undivided devotion, O Arjuna, can I in this form be known and truly seen and entered into, O scorcher of foes.

**6.33.55**  He who does my work, who holds me supreme, who is devoted to me, freed from attachment, without enmity toward any being — he comes to me, O Pāṇḍava.

**6.34.1**  Arjuna said: Those devotees who, ever steadfast, thus worship you, and those who worship the Imperishable, the Unmanifest — which of these are the better versed in yoga?

**6.34.2**  The Blessed Lord said: Those who fix their minds on me and, ever steadfast, worship me, endowed with supreme faith — these I deem the most perfect in yoga.

**6.34.3**  But those who worship the Imperishable, the indefinable, the Unmanifest, the all-pervading and unthinkable, the changeless, the immovable, the eternal,

**6.34.4**  restraining the whole host of the senses, even-minded toward all, rejoicing in the welfare of all beings — they too attain me.

**6.34.5**  Greater is the toil of those whose minds are fixed upon the Unmanifest; for the goal of the Unmanifest is hard for the embodied to reach.

**6.34.6**  But those who, renouncing all their actions in me and holding me supreme, worship me, meditating on me with undivided yoga —

**6.34.7**  of these, whose thought is set on me, I become ere long the deliverer from the ocean of death-bound transmigration, O Pārtha.

**6.34.8**  Fix your mind on me alone, let your understanding rest in me; thereafter you shall dwell in me alone — of this there is no doubt.

**6.34.9**  But if you cannot fix your thought steadily upon me, then seek to reach me through the yoga of repeated practice, O Dhanaṃjaya.

**6.34.10**  If you are unable even in practice, be intent on works done for my sake; doing your deeds for my sake, you shall attain perfection.

**6.34.11**  And if you are unable to do even this, then, taking refuge in union with me, renounce the fruit of all action, with the self subdued.

**6.34.12**  For knowledge is better than practice, and meditation surpasses knowledge, and the renunciation of the fruit of action is better than meditation; from renunciation peace follows at once.

**6.34.13**  He who hates no being, who is friendly and compassionate, free from the sense of 'mine' and from egoism, the same in pain and pleasure, forbearing,

**6.34.14**  ever content, the yogin, self-controlled, of firm resolve, his mind and understanding offered up to me — such a devotee of mine is dear to me.

**6.34.15**  He from whom the world does not shrink, and who does not shrink from the world, who is freed from elation, impatience, fear, and agitation — he too is dear to me.

**6.34.16**  Free from expectation, pure, capable, indifferent, untroubled, renouncing every undertaking — such a devotee of mine is dear to me.

**6.34.17**  He who neither rejoices nor hates, neither grieves nor desires, who has renounced both the fair and the foul, and is full of devotion — he is dear to me.

**6.34.18**  The same to foe and to friend, the same in honor and dishonor, the same in cold and heat, in pleasure and pain, freed from attachment,

**6.34.19**  alike in blame and praise, silent, content with whatever comes, with no fixed abode, steadfast in mind, full of devotion — that man is dear to me.

**6.34.20**  But those who honor this nectar of dharma as it has been declared, full of faith, holding me supreme — those devotees are exceedingly dear to me.

**6.35.1**  The Blessed Lord said: This body, O son of Kuntī, is called the field; and him who knows it, those who know such things call the knower of the field.

**6.35.2**  Know me too as the knower of the field in all fields, O Bhārata. The knowledge of the field and its knower — that I hold to be true knowledge.

**6.35.3**  What that field is, and of what nature, and with what modifications, and whence it arises; and who he is, and what his powers — hear this from me in brief.

**6.35.4**  It has been sung in many ways by the seers, severally in the diverse Vedic hymns, and also in the words of the aphorisms on Brahman, reasoned and well-determined.

**6.35.5**  The great elements, egoism, the intellect, and the Unmanifest; the eleven senses, and the five objects of the senses;

**6.35.6**  desire, aversion, pleasure, pain, the aggregate, consciousness, steadfastness — this, in brief, is the field declared together with its modifications.

**6.35.7**  Humility, freedom from pretense, non-violence, forbearance, uprightness, service to one's teacher, purity, steadiness, self-restraint;

**6.35.8**  dispassion toward the objects of sense, and absence of egoism; insight into the evil of birth, death, old age, sickness, and pain;

**6.35.9**  non-attachment, freedom from clinging to son, wife, home, and the rest; and constant evenness of mind amid the befalling of the desired and the undesired;

**6.35.10**  unswerving devotion to me through undivided yoga, resort to solitary places, and distaste for the throng of people;

**6.35.11**  constancy in the knowledge of the Self, and insight into the goal of the knowledge of truth — this is declared to be knowledge; and ignorance is whatever differs from this.

**6.35.12**  I shall now declare that which is to be known, knowing which one attains immortality: the supreme Brahman, without beginning, of which it is said neither that it is existent nor non-existent.

**6.35.13**  With hands and feet on every side, with eyes, heads, and faces on every side, with ears on every side, it stands enveloping all things in the world.

**6.35.14**  Shining through the functions of all the senses, yet devoid of all the senses; unattached, yet the support of all; free from the strands of nature, yet their enjoyer.

**6.35.15**  Without and within all beings, unmoving yet moving; by reason of its subtlety it is unknowable, and it is far off and yet near.

**6.35.16**  Undivided, yet abiding as though divided among beings; that which is to be known is the sustainer of beings, their devourer, and their creator.

**6.35.17**  It is the light even of lights, said to be beyond all darkness; it is knowledge, the object of knowledge, and the goal of knowledge, seated in the heart of all.

**6.35.18**  Thus the field, and likewise knowledge, and that which is to be known, have been told in brief. My devotee, understanding this, attains to my being.

**6.35.19**  Know that both Nature and the Spirit are without beginning; and know that the modifications and the strands of nature arise from Nature.

**6.35.20**  In the production of effect and cause and agency, Nature is said to be the cause; the Spirit is said to be the cause in the experiencing of pleasures and pains.

**6.35.21**  For the Spirit, seated in Nature, experiences the strands born of Nature; attachment to the strands is the cause of its births in good and evil wombs.

**6.35.22**  The witness, the consenter, the sustainer, the enjoyer, the Great Lord, and also called the Supreme Self — such is the supreme Spirit within this body.

**6.35.23**  He who thus knows the Spirit and Nature together with its strands, in whatever state he may live, is not born again.

**6.35.24**  By meditation some behold the Self in the self by the self; others by the yoga of knowledge, and yet others by the yoga of action.

**6.35.25**  But others, not knowing thus, worship having heard it from others; they too, devoted to what they have heard, cross beyond death.

**6.35.26**  Whatever being comes into existence, whether unmoving or moving, know that it arises from the union of the field and the knower of the field, O bull of the Bharatas.

**6.35.27**  He who sees the Supreme Lord abiding alike in all beings, the imperishable amid the perishing — he truly sees.

**6.35.28**  For seeing the Lord present alike everywhere, he does not destroy the Self by the self, and thereby he attains the supreme goal.

**6.35.29**  He who sees that actions are in every way performed by Nature alone, and the Self as the non-doer — he truly sees.

**6.35.30**  When he perceives the separate existence of beings as resting in the One, and their expansion from that alone, then he attains to Brahman.

**6.35.31**  Being beginningless and free from the strands of nature, this imperishable Supreme Self, though dwelling in the body, O son of Kuntī, neither acts nor is tainted.

**6.35.32**  As the all-pervading ether, by reason of its subtlety, is never tainted, so the Self, abiding everywhere in the body, is never tainted.

**6.35.33**  As the single sun illumines this whole world, so does the Field-knower illumine the entire Field, O Bhārata.

**6.35.34**  Those who thus discern, with the eye of knowledge, the difference between the Field and the Field-knower, and the release of beings from material nature—they attain the Supreme.

**6.36.1**  The Blessed Lord said: Once more I shall declare the supreme knowledge, the highest of all knowings, by knowing which all the sages have passed from here to the highest perfection.

**6.36.2**  Having taken refuge in this knowledge and come to share in My own nature, they are not born again even at a new creation, nor are they disturbed at the dissolution.

**6.36.3**  My womb is the great Brahman; in it I cast the seed. From that, O Bhārata, comes the birth of all beings.

**6.36.4**  Whatever forms take birth in all the wombs, O son of Kuntī, the great Brahman is their womb, and I am the seed-giving father.

**6.36.5**  Goodness, passion, and darkness—these are the qualities born of material nature, O mighty-armed; they bind the imperishable embodied one within the body.

**6.36.6**  Of these, goodness, being pure, is luminous and free from ill; it binds, O sinless one, through attachment to happiness and through attachment to knowledge.

**6.36.7**  Know passion to be of the nature of craving, arising from thirst and attachment; it binds the embodied one, O son of Kuntī, through attachment to action.

**6.36.8**  But darkness, know, is born of ignorance and deludes all embodied beings; it binds, O Bhārata, through heedlessness, sloth, and sleep.

**6.36.9**  Goodness binds one to happiness, O Bhārata, and passion to action; but darkness, veiling knowledge, binds one to heedlessness.

**6.36.10**  Goodness prevails by overpowering passion and darkness, O Bhārata; and passion overpowers goodness and darkness, while darkness overpowers goodness and passion.

**6.36.11**  When through all the gates of this body the light of knowledge arises, then one should know that goodness has grown strong.

**6.36.12**  Greed, activity, the undertaking of works, restlessness, and longing—these arise when passion has grown strong, O bull of the Bharatas.

**6.36.13**  Darkness, inertia, heedlessness, and delusion—these arise when darkness has grown strong, O joy of the Kurus.

**6.36.14**  When the embodied one meets dissolution while goodness is strong, he then attains the spotless worlds of those who know the highest.

**6.36.15**  Meeting dissolution in passion, one is born among those attached to action; and dissolving in darkness, one is born in the wombs of the deluded.

**6.36.16**  They say the fruit of good action is of the nature of goodness, pure; but the fruit of passion is suffering, and the fruit of darkness is ignorance.

**6.36.17**  From goodness arises knowledge, and from passion greed; from darkness arise heedlessness, delusion, and ignorance.

**6.36.18**  Those established in goodness go upward; the passionate remain in the middle; and those who dwell in the workings of the lowest quality, the darkened, go downward.

**6.36.19**  When the seer perceives no agent other than the qualities, and knows that which is beyond the qualities, he attains to My being.

**6.36.20**  Transcending these three qualities that spring from the body, the embodied one, freed from birth, death, old age, and sorrow, attains immortality.

**6.36.21**  Arjuna said: By what marks is one known who has transcended these three qualities, O Lord? What is his conduct, and how does he pass beyond these three qualities?

**6.36.22**  The Blessed Lord said: He who hates not illumination, activity, or delusion when they arise, O Pāṇḍava, nor longs for them when they cease;

**6.36.23**  who, seated as though indifferent, is not disturbed by the qualities; who, thinking only "the qualities are at work," stands firm and does not waver;

**6.36.24**  who is alike in sorrow and joy, self-poised, regarding clod, stone, and gold as equal; who is steadfast, the same toward the dear and the unwelcome, the same in blame and in praise of himself;

**6.36.25**  the same in honor and dishonor, the same toward the side of friend and of foe, renouncing all undertakings—he is said to have transcended the qualities.

**6.36.26**  And he who serves Me with unswerving devotion in yoga—he, transcending these qualities, is fit to become Brahman.

**6.36.27**  For I am the ground of Brahman, of the immortal and imperishable, of the eternal dharma, and of absolute bliss.

**6.37.1**  The Blessed Lord said: They speak of an imperishable aśvattha tree, with roots above and branches below, whose leaves are the hymns; he who knows it is a knower of the Vedas.

**6.37.2**  Its branches stretch below and above, nourished by the qualities, with sense-objects as their twigs; and below, its roots reach onward, binding to action in the world of men.

**6.37.3**  Its form is not perceived here as it truly is—it has no end, no beginning, no foundation. Having cut down this aśvattha with its firmly grown roots, by the strong axe of detachment,

**6.37.4**  then that goal is to be sought, gaining which one returns no more: "I take refuge in that primal Person from whom the ancient streaming-forth has flowed."

**6.37.5**  Free from pride and delusion, having conquered the fault of attachment, ever devoted to the supreme Self, with desires stilled, released from the dualities known as pleasure and pain—the undeluded go to that imperishable goal.

**6.37.6**  Neither sun nor moon nor fire illumines that, gaining which they return no more—that is My supreme abode.

**6.37.7**  An eternal portion of My very Self, become a living soul in the world of the living, draws to itself the senses, with mind as the sixth, that abide in material nature.

**6.37.8**  When the lord of the body takes on a body, and again when he departs from it, he takes these along and moves on, as the wind bears scents from their source.

**6.37.9**  Presiding over hearing, sight, touch, taste, and smell, and over the mind as well, this one enjoys the objects of the senses.

**6.37.10**  Whether departing, abiding, or enjoying, accompanied by the qualities, the deluded do not perceive him; but those with the eye of knowledge perceive him.

**6.37.11**  The yogins, striving, behold him established in the self; but the unrefined of soul, the mindless, though they strive, do not behold him.

**6.37.12**  The radiance that resides in the sun and illumines the whole world, and that which is in the moon and in fire—know that radiance to be Mine.

**6.37.13**  Entering the earth, I sustain all beings by My energy; and becoming the sap-filled Soma, I nourish all the plants.

**6.37.14**  Becoming the fire Vaiśvānara, I dwell in the bodies of living creatures and, joined with the in-breath and out-breath, I digest the four kinds of food.

**6.37.15**  And I am seated in the heart of all; from Me come memory, knowledge, and their loss. By all the Vedas I alone am to be known; I am the author of Vedānta and the knower of the Vedas.

**6.37.16**  There are these two persons in the world, the perishable and the imperishable. The perishable is all beings; the unchanging is called the imperishable.

**6.37.17**  But the Supreme Person is another, declared to be the highest Self, who, entering the three worlds, sustains them—the imperishable Lord.

**6.37.18**  Since I transcend the perishable and am higher even than the imperishable, therefore in the world and in the Veda I am renowned as the Supreme Person.

**6.37.19**  He who, undeluded, knows Me thus as the Supreme Person—he, knowing all, worships Me with his whole being, O Bhārata.

**6.37.20**  Thus this most secret teaching has been declared by Me, O sinless one. Awakening to this, a man becomes wise and has accomplished all there is to do, O Bhārata.

**6.38.1**  The Blessed Lord said: Fearlessness, purity of heart, steadfastness in the yoga of knowledge, charity, self-restraint, sacrifice, study of the scriptures, austerity, and uprightness;

**6.38.2**  non-violence, truth, freedom from anger, renunciation, peace, freedom from slander, compassion toward beings, freedom from greed, gentleness, modesty, and steadiness;

**6.38.3**  vigor, forgiveness, fortitude, purity, freedom from malice, and freedom from overweening pride—these belong, O Bhārata, to one born to the divine endowment.

**6.38.4**  Hypocrisy, arrogance, conceit, anger, harshness, and ignorance—these belong, O Pārtha, to one born to the demonic endowment.

**6.38.5**  The divine endowment is held to lead to liberation, the demonic to bondage. Grieve not, O Pāṇḍava; you are born to the divine endowment.

**6.38.6**  There are two orders of created beings in this world, the divine and the demonic. The divine has been described at length; now hear from Me, O Pārtha, of the demonic.

**6.38.7**  Demonic men know neither action nor abstention from action; in them there is found neither purity nor good conduct nor truth.

**6.38.8**  They say the world is without truth, without foundation, without a Lord, brought into being by no orderly mutual cause—what else but born of desire?

**6.38.9**  Holding fast to this view, ruined in soul and feeble in understanding, they rise up as enemies of the world, doers of fierce deeds, bent on its destruction.

**6.38.10**  Clinging to insatiable desire, filled with hypocrisy, conceit, and pride, having seized through delusion upon false notions, they act with impure resolves.

**6.38.11**  Beset by measureless cares that end only with death, devoted to the enjoyment of desires as the highest aim, convinced that this is all there is;

**6.38.12**  bound by a hundred cords of hope, given over to desire and anger, they strive to amass wealth by unjust means for the sake of sensual enjoyment.

**6.38.13**  "This I have gained today; this longing of my heart I shall attain; this is mine, and this wealth too shall be mine hereafter."

**6.38.14**  "That foe I have slain, and others too I shall slay. I am the lord, I am the enjoyer, I am successful, mighty, and happy."

**6.38.15**  "I am rich and high-born; who else is equal to me? I shall sacrifice, I shall give, I shall rejoice"—thus deluded by ignorance,

**6.38.16**  bewildered by many thoughts, enmeshed in the net of delusion, addicted to the enjoyment of desires, they fall into a foul hell.

**6.38.17**  Self-conceited, unbending, filled with the pride and intoxication of wealth, they perform sacrifices that are sacrifices in name only, with hypocrisy and without due rite.

**6.38.18**  Given over to egotism, force, arrogance, desire, and anger, these spiteful men hate Me, who dwell within their own bodies and the bodies of others.

**6.38.19**  These haters, cruel and vile among men, the most degraded, I hurl ceaselessly, those evil ones, into demonic wombs amid the rounds of rebirth.

**6.38.20**  Fallen into demonic wombs, deluded birth after birth, never reaching Me, O son of Kuntī, they thence go to the lowest course.

**6.38.21**  Threefold is this gate of hell, the ruin of the self—desire, anger, and greed; therefore one should abandon these three.

**6.38.22**  Released from these three gates of darkness, O son of Kuntī, a man works for the good of his soul and thence goes to the supreme course.

**6.38.23**  He who, casting aside the ordinance of scripture, acts under the impulse of desire attains neither perfection nor happiness nor the supreme course.

**6.38.24**  Therefore let scripture be your authority in determining what is to be done and what is not; knowing what is declared by the ordinance of scripture, you should act here in this world.

**6.39.1**  Arjuna said: Those who, casting aside the ordinance of scripture, yet sacrifice filled with faith—what is their standing, O Kṛṣṇa? Is it goodness, passion, or darkness?

**6.39.2**  The Blessed Lord said: The faith of embodied beings is of three kinds, born of their own nature—of goodness, of passion, and of darkness. Hear of it.

**6.39.3**  The faith of each is in accord with his nature, O Bhārata. Man is made of his faith; as his faith is, so is he.

**6.39.4**  Those of goodness worship the gods; those of passion worship the yakṣas and rākṣasas; and the others, men of darkness, worship the departed and the hordes of spirits.

**6.39.5**  Those men who practice grim austerity not ordained by scripture, joined to hypocrisy and egotism, driven by the force of desire and passion,

**6.39.6**  torturing, in their senselessness, the host of elements within the body, and Me too who dwell within the body—know them to be of demonic resolve.

**6.39.7**  The food, too, that is dear to each is of three kinds, and so are sacrifice, austerity, and giving. Hear now of their distinction.

**6.39.8**  Foods that promote length of life, vitality, strength, health, ease, and delight, that are savory, smooth, substantial, and gladdening to the heart — such are dear to those of sāttvika nature.

**6.39.9**  Foods that are bitter, sour, salty, exceedingly hot, pungent, harsh, and burning are sought by the man of rajas; they bring pain, sorrow, and disease.

**6.39.10**  Food that is stale, drained of its savor, putrid, kept overnight, the leavings of others, and unfit for offering — such is dear to the man of tamas.

**6.39.11**  That sacrifice which is offered according to the ordinance by those who crave no fruit, the mind being settled in the thought 'this is to be sacrificed and nothing more' — that sacrifice is sāttvika.

**6.39.12**  But that sacrifice which is offered with an eye to its fruit, and likewise for the sake of display — know that sacrifice, O best of the Bharatas, to be rājasa.

**6.39.13**  A sacrifice devoid of ordinance, with no food distributed, lacking the sacred verses, without priestly gifts, and destitute of faith — that they declare to be tāmasa.

**6.39.14**  Reverence shown to the gods, the twice-born, teachers, and the wise; purity, uprightness, continence, and non-injury — this is called austerity of the body.

**6.39.15**  Speech that causes no agitation, that is truthful, pleasant, and beneficial, together with the constant practice of sacred recitation — this is called austerity of speech.

**6.39.16**  Serenity of mind, gentleness, silence, self-restraint, and purity of disposition — this is called austerity of the mind.

**6.39.17**  This threefold austerity, practiced with the highest faith by disciplined men who crave no fruit, is declared to be sāttvika.

**6.39.18**  That austerity which is performed for the sake of respect, honor, and reverence, and out of ostentation, is here declared rājasa — unsteady and impermanent.

**6.39.19**  That austerity which is performed through a deluded notion, with self-torture, or for the purpose of ruining another, is declared to be tāmasa.

**6.39.20**  That gift which is given with the thought 'it ought to be given,' to one who can make no return, in a fit place and time and to a worthy recipient — that gift is held to be sāttvika.

**6.39.21**  But that gift which is given for the sake of a return, or with an eye to its fruit, or grudgingly — that gift is held to be rājasa.

**6.39.22**  That gift which is given in the wrong place and time and to unworthy persons, without due respect and with contempt, is declared to be tāmasa.

**6.39.23**  'Oṃ Tat Sat' — this is held to be the threefold designation of Brahman; by it of old were ordained the brāhmaṇas, the Vedas, and the sacrifices.

**6.39.24**  Therefore, with the utterance of 'Oṃ,' the acts of sacrifice, gift, and austerity prescribed by ordinance ever proceed among those who expound Brahman.

**6.39.25**  With the word 'Tat,' and without aiming at any fruit, the various acts of sacrifice and austerity and the acts of giving are performed by those who seek release (mokṣa).

**6.39.26**  In the sense of 'reality' and of 'goodness' the word 'Sat' is employed; and likewise, O Pārtha, the word 'Sat' is applied to an auspicious deed.

**6.39.27**  Steadfastness in sacrifice, in austerity, and in giving is called 'Sat'; and an action done for the sake of That is likewise designated 'Sat.'

**6.39.28**  Whatever is offered, given, or undertaken as austerity, and whatever is done without faith, is called 'Asat,' O Pārtha; it avails nothing hereafter, nor here.

**6.40.1**  Arjuna said: I wish to know, O mighty-armed one, the truth of renunciation (saṃnyāsa) and likewise, distinctly, of relinquishment (tyāga), O Hṛṣīkeśa, O slayer of Keśin.

**6.40.2**  The Blessed Lord said: The seers understand by renunciation the laying aside of acts done from desire; the discerning declare the relinquishment of the fruit of all actions to be relinquishment.

**6.40.3**  Some thoughtful men declare that action is to be relinquished as a thing of evil; others, that acts of sacrifice, gift, and austerity are not to be relinquished.

**6.40.4**  Hear my conclusion on this matter of relinquishment, O best of the Bharatas; for relinquishment, O tiger among men, is declared to be of three kinds.

**6.40.5**  Acts of sacrifice, gift, and austerity are not to be relinquished; they must indeed be performed. Sacrifice, gift, and austerity are purifiers of the thoughtful.

**6.40.6**  Yet even these actions are to be performed having abandoned attachment and their fruits — this, O Pārtha, is my settled and supreme conviction.

**6.40.7**  But the renunciation of a prescribed action is not fitting; its abandonment through delusion is declared to be tāmasa.

**6.40.8**  He who relinquishes an action merely because it is painful, from fear of bodily affliction, makes a rājasa relinquishment, and gains none of the fruit of relinquishment.

**6.40.9**  When a prescribed action is performed simply because it ought to be done, O Arjuna, having abandoned attachment and the fruit as well — that relinquishment is held to be sāttvika.

**6.40.10**  He does not loathe disagreeable work, nor cling to agreeable work — the relinquisher, suffused with sattva, wise, and his doubts cut away.

**6.40.11**  For it is not possible for an embodied being to relinquish actions entirely; but he who relinquishes the fruit of action is called a relinquisher.

**6.40.12**  The fruit of action is of three kinds — unwished, wished, and mixed — and it accrues after death to those who have not relinquished, but never to the renouncers.

**6.40.13**  Learn from me, O mighty-armed one, these five causes, declared in the Sāṃkhya doctrine that brings actions to their end, for the accomplishment of all works.

**6.40.14**  The seat of action, likewise the agent, and the instruments of various kinds, the manifold and distinct exertions, and divine providence as the fifth among them.

**6.40.15**  Whatever action a man undertakes with body, speech, or mind, be it right or wrong, these five are its causes.

**6.40.16**  This being so, he who, through an untrained understanding, looks upon the self alone as the agent — that man of perverse mind does not truly see.

**6.40.17**  He whose disposition is free of the sense of 'I,' whose understanding is not stained — even though he slay these worlds, he slays not, nor is he bound.

**6.40.18**  Knowledge, the object of knowledge, and the knower — this is the threefold impulse to action; the instrument, the act, and the agent — this is the threefold sum of action.

**6.40.19**  Knowledge, action, and the agent are each declared to be of three kinds according to the distinction of the guṇas; hear of these too, duly, in the enumeration of the qualities.

**6.40.20**  That knowledge by which one beholds in all beings a single, imperishable Being, undivided amid the divided — know that knowledge to be sāttvika.

**6.40.21**  But that knowledge which, by reason of separateness, perceives in all beings manifold existences of distinct kinds — know that knowledge to be rājasa.

**6.40.22**  But that which clings to a single effect as though it were the whole, without reason, having no grasp of the real, and trifling — that is declared to be tāmasa.

**6.40.23**  An action that is prescribed, free of attachment, done without passion or aversion by one who seeks no fruit — that is called sāttvika.

**6.40.24**  But that action which is performed by one seeking to gratify desire, or again with the sense of 'I,' and with great toil — that is declared to be rājasa.

**6.40.25**  That action which is undertaken through delusion, with no regard for its consequence, for loss, for injury, or for one's own capacity — that is called tāmasa.

**6.40.26**  Free from attachment, not speaking of himself, endowed with steadfastness and zeal, unmoved in success and failure — such an agent is called sāttvika.

**6.40.27**  Passionate, eager for the fruit of action, greedy, of a violent nature, impure, swayed by joy and grief — such an agent is declared to be rājasa.

**6.40.28**  Undisciplined, vulgar, stubborn, deceitful, malicious, slothful, despondent, and procrastinating — such an agent is called tāmasa.

**6.40.29**  Hear now, O Dhanaṃjaya, the threefold distinction of understanding and of steadfastness according to the guṇas, set forth fully and severally.

**6.40.30**  That understanding which knows action and abstention, what is to be done and what is not, fear and fearlessness, bondage and release — that, O Pārtha, is sāttvika.

**6.40.31**  That understanding by which one discerns wrongly right and wrong, and what is to be done and what is not — that understanding, O Pārtha, is rājasa.

**6.40.32**  That understanding which, enveloped in darkness, deems wrong to be right and sees all things reversed — that, O Pārtha, is tāmasa.

**6.40.33**  That steadfastness by which, through unwavering yoga, one holds fast the workings of the mind, the breaths, and the senses — that, O Pārtha, is sāttvika.

**6.40.34**  But that steadfastness by which, O Arjuna, one clings to duty, pleasure, and wealth, craving their fruit with attachment — that, O Pārtha, is rājasa.

**6.40.35**  That steadfastness by which a dull-witted man does not let go of sleep, fear, grief, despondency, and conceit — that, O Pārtha, is tāmasa.

**6.40.36**  Now hear from me, O bull of the Bharatas, of the threefold happiness — that in which one delights through practice and comes to the end of sorrow.

**6.40.37**  That which at first is like poison but in its ripening is like nectar — that happiness, born of the serenity of one's own understanding of the self, is declared sāttvika.

**6.40.38**  That which, born of the contact of the senses with their objects, is at first like nectar but in its ripening is like poison — that happiness is held to be rājasa.

**6.40.39**  That happiness which both at first and in its sequel deludes the self, arising from sleep, sloth, and heedlessness — that is declared to be tāmasa.

**6.40.40**  There is no being on earth, nor again in heaven among the gods, that could be free from these three guṇas born of Prakṛti.

**6.40.41**  The duties of brāhmaṇas, kṣatriyas, and vaiśyas, and of śūdras too, O scorcher of foes, are apportioned according to the guṇas that spring from their own nature.

**6.40.42**  Calm, self-control, austerity, purity, forbearance, and uprightness, knowledge, discernment, and faith in what is — these are the duties of the brāhmaṇa, born of his own nature.

**6.40.43**  Heroism, majesty, steadfastness, skill, and not fleeing in battle, generosity, and a lordly bearing — these are the duties of the kṣatriya, born of his own nature.

**6.40.44**  Tillage, the tending of cattle, and trade are the duties of the vaiśya, born of his own nature; and the śūdra's duty, born of his nature too, consists of service.

**6.40.45**  Devoted each to his own duty, a man attains perfection. Hear now how one intent on his own work finds that perfection.

**6.40.46**  That One from whom the activity of all beings springs, by whom all this is pervaded — worshiping Him through his own work, a man finds perfection.

**6.40.47**  Better one's own duty, though imperfect, than the duty of another well performed. Performing the work ordained by one's own nature, a man incurs no guilt.

**6.40.48**  One should not abandon, O son of Kuntī, the work to which one is born, even though it be flawed; for all undertakings are veiled by flaw, as fire by smoke.

**6.40.49**  He whose understanding is unattached everywhere, who has mastered himself and from whom longing has departed, attains through renunciation the supreme perfection of actionlessness.

**6.40.50**  Learn from me in brief, O son of Kuntī, how, having attained perfection, one reaches Brahman — that which is the highest consummation of knowledge.

**6.40.51**  Endowed with a purified understanding, restraining the self with steadfastness, abandoning the objects of sense beginning with sound, and casting away passion and aversion;

**6.40.52**  frequenting solitude, eating but little, with speech, body, and mind controlled, ever devoted to the yoga of meditation, taking refuge in dispassion;

**6.40.53**  having let go of egoism, force, arrogance, desire, anger, and possession, free from the sense of 'mine' and at peace — he becomes fit for oneness with Brahman.

**6.40.54**  Become Brahman, serene in self, he neither grieves nor craves; alike toward all beings, he gains supreme devotion to Me.

**6.40.55**  Through devotion he comes to know Me — how great I am and who I am in truth; then, having known Me in truth, he enters into Me forthwith.

**6.40.56**  Though ever performing all actions, taking refuge in Me, he attains by My grace the eternal, imperishable abode.

**6.40.57**  Renouncing in thought all actions unto Me, intent on Me, having recourse to the yoga of understanding, be ever fixed in thought upon Me.

**6.40.58**  With your thought on Me, by My grace you shall cross over all difficulties; but if from egoism you will not heed, you shall perish.

**6.40.59**  If, clinging to egoism, you think 'I will not fight,' vain is this resolve of yours; your own nature will compel you.

**6.40.60**  Bound, O son of Kuntī, by your own deeds born of your innate nature, that very thing which in delusion you wish not to do, you shall do even against your will.

**6.40.61**  The Lord, O Arjuna, dwells in the heart-region of all beings, whirling all beings round by his māyā as though they were mounted upon a machine.

**6.40.62**  To him alone go for refuge with your whole being, O Bhārata; by his grace you shall attain the supreme peace and the eternal abode.

**6.40.63**  Thus has the knowledge, more secret than all that is secret, been declared to you by me. Ponder it fully, and then act as you will.

**6.40.64**  Hear again my supreme word, the most secret of all. Because you are dearly beloved by me, I shall now declare to you what is for your good.

**6.40.65**  Fix your mind on me, be devoted to me, sacrifice to me, bow down to me. To me alone you shall come; truly I promise you this, for you are dear to me.

**6.40.66**  Abandoning all duties, come to me alone for refuge. I shall liberate you from all sins; grieve not.

**6.40.67**  This is never to be told by you to one who practices no austerity, nor to one without devotion, nor to one who does not wish to listen, nor to one who scoffs at me.

**6.40.68**  Whoever shall declare this supreme secret among my devotees, rendering the highest devotion to me, shall come to me alone, without doubt.

**6.40.69**  And among men there shall be none who renders me more cherished service than he, nor shall there be another on earth dearer to me than he.

**6.40.70**  And whoever shall study this sacred dialogue of ours, by him I shall be worshipped through the sacrifice of knowledge: such is my conviction.

**6.40.71**  And the man who, full of faith and free from spite, should merely listen to it—he too, set free, shall attain the blessed worlds of those whose deeds are meritorious.

**6.40.72**  Has this been heard by you, O Pārtha, with a mind fixed upon one point? Has the delusion born of ignorance been destroyed in you, O Dhanaṃjaya?

**6.40.73**  Arjuna said: My delusion is destroyed, and through your grace, O Acyuta, I have regained my memory. I stand firm, my doubts dispelled; I will do your word.

**6.40.74**  Saṃjaya said: Thus did I hear this marvelous dialogue, making the hair stand on end, between Vāsudeva and the great-souled Pārtha.

**6.40.75**  By the grace of Vyāsa I have heard this supreme secret, this yoga, from Kṛṣṇa, the lord of yoga, declaring it in person and with his own voice.

**6.40.76**  O king, recalling again and again this wondrous and holy dialogue of Keśava and Arjuna, I thrill with joy time after time.

**6.40.77**  And recalling again and again that exceedingly marvelous form of Hari, great is my wonder, O king, and I rejoice over and over.

**6.40.78**  Wherever there is Kṛṣṇa, the lord of yoga, wherever there is Pārtha the bowman, there are fortune, victory, prosperity, and steadfast righteousness: such is my conviction.

**6.41.1**  Saṃjaya said: Then, beholding Dhanaṃjaya bearing his arrows and the Gāṇḍīva bow, the great chariot-warriors once again let loose a mighty roar.

**6.41.2**  The Pāṇḍavas and the Somakas, and those who followed them, gladdened heroes, blew their conches, born of the sea.

**6.41.3**  Then kettledrums and tabors, krakaca-trumpets and cow-horns were all struck at once, and a tremendous din arose.

**6.41.4**  Then the gods together with the gandharvas and the ancestors, O lord of men, and the hosts of siddhas and cāraṇas, gathered there eager to behold the spectacle.

**6.41.5**  And the highly blessed sages, placing Śatakratu at their head, assembled there together to witness that great slaughter.

**6.41.6**  Then Yudhiṣṭhira, beholding those two armies, vast as the ocean, roused and arrayed for battle and surging again and again, O king—

**6.41.7**  the hero loosed his armor, laid aside his excellent weapon, swiftly descended from his chariot, and on foot, with hands joined in reverence—

**6.41.8**  gazing toward his grandsire, Yudhiṣṭhira the king of righteousness, with words restrained, set forth, facing eastward, toward the host of the foe.

**6.41.9**  Beholding him setting forth, Dhanaṃjaya the son of Kuntī swiftly stepped down from his chariot and, together with his brothers, followed him.

**6.41.10**  And the lord Vāsudeva walked behind him, and the foremost kings too, full of eagerness, went after him.

**6.41.11**  Arjuna said: What have you resolved, O king, that leaving us behind you set out on foot, facing eastward toward the army of the enemy?

**6.41.12**  Bhīmasena said: Where will you go, O king of kings, with armor and weapons cast aside, while the enemy's hosts stand mailed, and forsaking your brothers, O lord of the earth?

**6.41.13**  Nakula said: When you, my eldest brother, go forth in this manner, O Bhārata, fear torments my heart. Tell me, whither do you mean to go?

**6.41.14**  Sahadeva said: While this dreadful press of battle is upon us and the fight is to be joined, where will you go, advancing thus toward the foe, O king?

**6.41.15**  Saṃjaya said: Though thus addressed by his brothers, O delight of the Kurus, Yudhiṣṭhira, his speech restrained, said nothing at all, but went straight on.

**6.41.16**  To them the greatly wise and high-minded Vāsudeva spoke, as though smiling: His purpose I have understood.

**6.41.17**  This king, having sought the leave of Bhīṣma and Droṇa, of Gautama and of Śalya, indeed of all his elders, will then do battle with his foes.

**6.41.18**  For it is heard that in the ancient ordinance whoever does battle without seeking the leave of his elders is plainly held in ill regard by those who are greater than he.

**6.41.19**  But he who, having sought their leave according to the scriptures, fights with those greater than himself—his is certain victory in war: such is my conviction.

**6.41.20**  As Kṛṣṇa spoke thus, in the army of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son there arose a great cry of dismay, while others among them fell silent.

**6.41.21**  Seeing Yudhiṣṭhira from afar, the soldiers of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son spoke to one another: This man is a disgrace to his line, undone.

**6.41.22**  Plainly, as if afraid, this king Yudhiṣṭhira approaches Bhīṣma, drawing near with his brothers, a suppliant begging for refuge.

**6.41.23**  With Dhanaṃjaya for protector, and Vṛkodara the Pāṇḍava, and Nakula and Sahadeva—how is it that the son of Pāṇḍu comes forward in fear?

**6.41.24**  Surely he was not born in a kṣatriya line renowned upon the earth, weak of spirit as he is, whose heart is thus stricken with terror in the fray.

**6.41.25**  Then all those kṣatriyas fell to praising the Kauravas and, become glad and joyful at heart, each waved his garments aloft.

**6.41.26**  Then all the warriors there reviled Yudhiṣṭhira, O lord of the people, together with his brothers and with Keśava.

**6.41.27**  Then that Kaurava host, having cried shame upon Yudhiṣṭhira, fell silent again all at once, O lord of the people.

**6.41.28**  What will this king say? What will Bhīṣma answer? What will Bhīma, who glories in battle, say? And what will Kṛṣṇa and Arjuna say?—thus they wondered.

**6.41.29**  What does he intend to say? A very great uncertainty then arose in both armies, O king, on account of Yudhiṣṭhira.

**6.41.30**  Plunging into the enemy's host, thronged with arrows and spears, he swiftly approached Bhīṣma, encircled by his brothers.

**6.41.31**  Then, clasping his feet with both hands, the Pāṇḍava king addressed Bhīṣma, son of Śaṃtanu, who stood ready for battle.

**6.41.32**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: I take my leave of you, O unassailable one; I shall fight against you, dear father. Grant me your leave, dear father, and bestow upon me your blessings.

**6.41.33**  Bhīṣma said: Had you not come to me thus before the battle, O lord of the earth, I should have cursed you, great king, unto your defeat, O Bhārata.

**6.41.34**  I am pleased, my son; fight, and win victory, O Pāṇḍava. Whatever else you desire, that too may you obtain in the battle.

**6.41.35**  And let a boon be chosen, O Pārtha; what do you wish from us? When matters stand thus, O great king, defeat shall not be yours.

**6.41.36**  A man is the slave of wealth, but wealth is no one's slave: this is the truth, O great king. By their wealth I am bound to the Kauravas.

**6.41.37**  Therefore I speak to you, O delight of the Kurus, words as of a eunuch: I have been bought with wealth, O Kaurava. What do you desire other than battle?

**6.41.38**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: Counsel me, O greatly wise one, ever seeking my good; fight for the Kaurava's sake. This is forever the boon I ask of you.

**6.41.39**  Bhīṣma said: O king, what help can I render you in this, O delight of the Kurus? Fight I shall, willingly, for another's sake; tell me what you wish to say.

**6.41.40**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: How may I conquer you, unvanquished, in the fray? Counsel me in this for my good, if you see what is best.

**6.41.41**  Bhīṣma said: I see no man, O son of Kuntī, who could vanquish me as I fight in the battle—not even Śatakratu himself in person.

**6.41.42**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: Then, grandsire, I ask you—homage be to you—tell me yourself the means by which you may be overcome by foes in battle.

**6.41.43**  Bhīṣma said: I see no enemy, my child, who could conquer me in battle; the hour of my death has not yet come. Come to me again.

**6.41.44**  Saṃjaya said: Then Yudhiṣṭhira, O delight of the Kurus, received Bhīṣma's words with bowed head and once more saluted him.

**6.41.45**  Then the mighty-armed one set forth again toward the teacher's chariot, in full view of all the armies, passing through their midst together with his brothers.

**6.41.46**  Having saluted Droṇa and made the rightward circumambulation about him, he spoke to that unassailable one a word that sought his own highest good.

**6.41.47**  I take my leave of you, O venerable one; I shall fight, freed of all guilt, and may I conquer all my foes when granted your leave, O brāhmaṇa.

**6.41.48**  Droṇa said: Had you not come to me, resolved upon battle, I should have cursed you wholly, great king, unto your utter defeat.

**6.41.49**  Therefore, Yudhiṣṭhira, I am content, and honored by you, O sinless one. I grant you leave: fight, and win the victory.

**6.41.50**  And I shall fulfill your desire; tell me what you long for. When matters stand thus, O great king, what do you wish other than battle?

**6.41.51**  A man is the slave of wealth, but wealth is no one's slave: this is the truth, O great king. By their wealth I am bound to the Kauravas.

**6.41.52**  Therefore I speak to you words as of a eunuch: what do you wish other than battle? I shall fight for the Kaurava's sake, yet your victory is to be wished for by me.

**6.41.53**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: Wish me victory, O brāhmaṇa, and counsel me for my good; fight for the Kaurava's sake. This is the boon I have chosen.

**6.41.54**  Droṇa said: Certain is your victory, O king, you whose counselor is Hari. And I assure you: in battle you shall conquer your foes.

**6.41.55**  Where there is righteousness, there is Kṛṣṇa; where there is Kṛṣṇa, there is victory. Fight, go forth, O son of Kuntī; ask of me—what shall I tell you?

**6.41.56**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: I ask you, O best of the twice-born; hear what I wish to say. How may I conquer you, unvanquished, in the fray?

**6.41.57**  Droṇa said: There is no victory for you so long as I fight in battle. Strive swiftly, O king, together with your brothers, for my slaying.

**6.41.58**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: Alas! Then tell me, O mighty-armed one, the means of your own destruction. Bowing low, O teacher, I ask you this—homage be to you.

**6.41.59**  Droṇa said: I see no enemy, my child, who could slay me as I stand in battle, fighting in full fury and pouring forth floods and torrents of arrows—

**6.41.60**  save when, O king, I have resolved to fast unto death, have laid down my weapons and grown senseless to the world—then among warriors one might slay me in battle. This truth I tell you.

**6.41.61**  And I shall cast away my weapons in the battle on hearing some most grievous tidings from a man whose word is to be trusted: this truth I tell you.

**6.41.62**  Saṃjaya said: Having heard these words of the wise son of Bharadvāja, O great king, and having taken leave of that teacher, he went toward the son of Śaradvat.

**6.41.63**  Saluting Kṛpa and walking around him in reverence, that king, skilled in speech, spoke this word to the most unassailable of men.

**6.41.64**  I ask your leave, O teacher; I shall fight, freed of all blame. With your sanction, O sinless one, may I conquer all my foes.

**6.41.65**  Kṛpa said: Had you not come to me, resolved upon the battle, I would have cursed you, O great king, to utter ruin in every way.

**6.41.66**  A man is the slave of wealth, but wealth is no man's slave. That is the truth, O great king: I am bound by wealth to the Kauravas.

**6.41.67**  For their sake I must fight—such is my resolve, O great king. Therefore I speak to you like one unmanned: what do you wish, other than battle?

**6.41.68**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: Then come, I shall ask you, O teacher; hear my words.

**6.41.69**  Saṃjaya said: Having spoken thus, the king grew troubled and, his senses faltering, said nothing more. But the descendant of Gotama, perceiving what he meant to ask, answered him: I cannot be slain, O lord of earth; fight, and win the victory.

**6.41.70**  Pleased by your coming, O ruler of men, I shall ever rise up and pray for your victory. This truth I tell you.

**6.41.71**  Having heard these words of the descendant of Gotama, O great king, and having taken leave of Kṛpa, the king went on toward the lord of the Madras.
