**6.105.24**  "Duryodhana, understand this and be steadfast, O lord of peoples. Long ago I made a vow to you, O mighty one:

**6.105.25**  that having slain ten thousand of the great-souled warriors I would withdraw from the field—this was to be my daily task; and that, O bull of the Bharatas, I have done as I promised.

**6.105.26**  Today too I shall perform a mighty deed in the great battle: either I shall lie slain, or I shall slay the Pāṇḍavas this day.

**6.105.27**  This day, O tiger among men, I shall discharge the great debt I owe for my master's bread, O king, by falling slain at the head of the host."

**6.105.28**  Having spoken thus, that best of the Bharatas, irresistible, scorching the warriors with his arrows, fell upon the army of the Pāṇḍavas.

**6.105.29**  As the son of Gaṅgā stood in the midst of the host, O bull of the Bharatas, wrathful like a venomous serpent, the Pāṇḍavas surrounded him on every side.

**6.105.30**  But on that tenth day, displaying his own might, O king, O joy of the Kurus, he slew hundreds of thousands.

**6.105.31**  And of those mighty princes who were the foremost of the Pāñcālas, he drank up the splendor, as the sun with its rays drinks up water.

**6.105.32**  Having slain ten thousand swift elephants with their riders, O great king, and ten thousand horses besides,

**6.105.33**  and full two hundred thousand foot-soldiers, that best of men, Bhīṣma, blazed forth in the battle like a smokeless fire.

**6.105.34**  And none among the Pāṇḍavas could even look upon him, as upon the sun blazing when it has entered the northern path.

**6.105.35**  Then the Pāṇḍavas, enraged and afflicted by that great archer, together with the Sṛñjaya chariot-warriors, rushed upon Bhīṣma to slay him.

**6.105.36**  As he fought against the many, Bhīṣma, son of Śāntanu, that mighty-armed one, was then covered over like a mountain by dark clouds.

**6.105.37**  But your sons, with a great army, surrounded the son of Gaṅgā on every side; and thereupon the battle was joined.

**6.106.1**  Saṃjaya said: But Arjuna, O king, seeing Bhīṣma's prowess in the battle, then said to Śikhaṇḍin: "Go forth against the grandsire.

**6.106.2**  And no fear at all need you feel of Bhīṣma this day; I shall bring him down from his splendid chariot with my sharp arrows."

**6.106.3**  Thus addressed by the Pārtha, O bull of the Bharatas, Śikhaṇḍin, hearing the Pārtha's words, charged upon the son of Gaṅgā.

**6.106.4**  So too, O king, Dhṛṣṭadyumna and the son of Subhadrā, that great chariot-warrior, hearing the Pārtha's words, rushed gladly upon Bhīṣma.

**6.106.5**  The aged Virāṭa and Drupada, and Kuntibhoja in his armor, charged upon the son of Gaṅgā while your son looked on.

**6.106.6**  Nakula and Sahadeva, and the valiant King of Righteousness, and all the other armies as well, O lord of peoples, hearing the Pārtha's words, rushed together upon the son of Gaṅgā.

**6.106.7**  And your warriors, assembled, advanced to meet those great chariot-warriors, each to the best of his strength and ardor; hear me as I recount it.

**6.106.8**  Citrasena, O great king, advanced against Cekitāna, who was moving in the battle eager to reach Bhīṣma, as a tiger-cub goes against a bull.

**6.106.9**  Dhṛṣṭadyumna, O great king, as he came near to Bhīṣma, intent and hastening in the battle, was checked by Kṛtavarman.

**6.106.10**  Bhīmasena, in great wrath and seeking the slaying of the son of Gaṅgā, was checked in haste, O great king, by Saumadatti.

**6.106.11**  Likewise the hero Nakula, scattering his many shafts, Vikarṇa warded off, desiring to save Bhīṣma's life.

**6.106.12**  And Sahadeva, as he pressed intent toward Bhīṣma's chariot, Kṛpa, son of Śaradvat, enraged, warded off in the fight.

**6.106.13**  Against the rākṣasa of cruel deeds, the son of Bhīmasena, mighty in strength and eager for Bhīṣma's death, the strong Durmukha charged.

**6.106.14**  Sātyaki, wrathful in the battle, Ārśyaśṛṅgi warded off; and Abhimanyu, O great king, as he advanced toward Bhīṣma's chariot, Sudakṣiṇa the Kāmboja, O great king, turned back.

**6.106.15**  The aged Virāṭa and Drupada, those two crushers of foes joined together, Aśvatthāman then in wrath held off, O Bhārata.

**6.106.16**  So too the eldest son of Pāṇḍu, who longed for Bhīṣma's slaying, the son of Bharadvāja, intent in the battle, held off—the son of Dharma.

**6.106.17**  Arjuna, impetuous in the fight, with Śikhaṇḍin placed before him, eager to reach Bhīṣma and scorching the ten directions, O great king, Duḥśāsana the great archer warded off in the engagement.

**6.106.18**  And other warriors of yours warded off in the battle the great chariot-warriors of the Pāṇḍavas as they advanced toward Bhīṣma.

**6.106.19**  But Dhṛṣṭadyumna cried out to the armies again and again: "Rush in fury upon Bhīṣma alone, the mighty one!

**6.106.20**  This Arjuna, joy of the Kurus, advances in the battle against Bhīṣma. Rush in, fear not; for Bhīṣma shall not reach you.

**6.106.21**  Not even Vāsava would dare to fight Arjuna in battle—how much less Bhīṣma, O heroes, whose strength is spent and whose life is short!"

**6.106.22**  Hearing this from their commander, the great chariot-warriors of the Pāṇḍavas rushed forward, delighted, toward the chariot of the son of Gaṅgā.

**6.106.23**  As they came on in the battle like mighty floods of water, your bulls among men, delighted, held them back.

**6.106.24**  Duḥśāsana, O great king, that great chariot-warrior, casting off fear and longing to save Bhīṣma's life, rushed upon Dhanaṃjaya.

**6.106.25**  Likewise the heroic Pāṇḍavas rushed in the engagement toward the chariot of the son of Gaṅgā, against your sons, the great chariot-warriors.

**6.106.26**  There we beheld a marvel of wondrous form, O lord of peoples: that the Pārtha, having reached Duḥśāsana's chariot, could not pass beyond it.

**6.106.27**  As the shore holds back the turbulent great ocean, so did your son hold back the wrathful Pāṇḍava.

**6.106.28**  For both were the foremost of chariot-warriors, both hard to conquer, O Bhārata; both were like the moon and the sun in beauty and in radiance, O Bhārata.

**6.106.29**  Both thus roused to fury, each longing for the other's death, they met in the great battle as Maya and Śakra met of old.

**6.106.30**  Duḥśāsana, O great king, struck the Pāṇḍava with three shafts and Vāsudeva with twenty in the engagement.

**6.106.31**  Then Arjuna, his anger kindled at the sight of the Vārṣṇeya struck, pierced Duḥśāsana in the fight with a hundred iron shafts; and they, cleaving his armor, drank his blood in the battle.

**6.106.32**  Then Duḥśāsana in wrath pierced the Pārtha in the forehead with five arrows whose joints were well bent, O best of the Bharatas.

**6.106.33**  With those arrows set in his forehead, that best of the Pāṇḍavas shone, O great king, like Meru with its peaks towering exceedingly high.

**6.106.34**  Sorely pierced by your son the bowman, that great archer, the Pārtha, glowed in the battle like a kiṃśuka tree in flower.

**6.106.35**  Then the Pāṇḍava in wrath afflicted Duḥśāsana, as fierce Rāhu in fury afflicts the moon on the day of conjunction.

**6.106.36**  Hard pressed by that mighty one, your son, O lord of peoples, pierced the Pārtha in the battle with heron-feathered shafts whetted on stone.

**6.106.37**  The valiant Pārtha, swiftly cutting his bow, then struck your son thereafter with nine arrows.

**6.106.38**  He, taking up another bow and standing before Bhīṣma, struck Arjuna with twenty-five shafts in the arms and on the breast.

**6.106.39**  Enraged at him, O great king, the Pāṇḍava, tormentor of foes, dispatched many terrible shafts like the rods of Yama.

**6.106.40**  Those arrows of the striving Pārtha your son cut down before they reached him—that seemed a marvel; and your son pierced the Pārtha with whetted shafts.

**6.106.41**  Then the Pārtha in wrath, fitting his arrows to the bow, loosed in the battle golden-feathered shafts whetted on stone.

**6.106.42**  Those arrows sank into the body of that great-souled one, O great king, even as swans, O descendant of Bharata, plunge into a pond they have reached.

**6.106.43**  Hard pressed by the great-souled Pāṇḍava, your son left Pārtha in the battle and swiftly took refuge with Bhīṣma's chariot; and as he was sinking in those fathomless depths, Bhīṣma became his island.

**6.106.44**  Then, regaining his senses, your son, O lord of peoples, that valiant hero, once more held his ground and showed his prowess.

**6.106.45**  With keenly whetted arrows that hero of great might pierced Pārtha, as Vṛtra once pierced Purandara, and he flinched not at all before Arjuna.

**6.107.1**  Saṃjaya said: As mailed Sātyaki rose up in the battle against Bhīṣma, the son of Ṛśyaśṛṅga, that mighty archer, held him in check in the fray.

**6.107.2**  But Mādhava, greatly enraged, struck the rākṣasa in the battle with nine arrows, O king, as if smiling the while, O descendant of Bharata.

**6.107.3**  Likewise the rākṣasa, O king, in his fury tormented Mādhava, that bull of the Śini line, with sharpened arrows, O king of kings.

**6.107.4**  Then the descendant of Śini, that Mādhava, slayer of enemy heroes, in great wrath sent forth a mass of arrows at the rākṣasa in the fray.

**6.107.5**  Thereupon the rākṣasa pierced the mighty-armed Sātyaki of true valor with keen arrows, and roared aloud with a lion's roar.

**6.107.6**  But Mādhava, though sorely wounded by the rākṣasa in the battle, that glorious one, holding fast to his steadiness, both laughed and roared.

**6.107.7**  Then Bhagadatta, enraged, struck Mādhava in the battle with sharpened arrows, as one goads a great elephant with the spiked driving-hooks.

**6.107.8**  Leaving the rākṣasa in the combat, the descendant of Śini, foremost of chariot-warriors, hurled smooth-jointed arrows at the lord of Prāgjyotiṣa.

**6.107.9**  The king of Prāgjyotiṣa, with a sharp-edged broad-headed shaft, cut asunder the great bow of Mādhava, as one skilled of hand.

**6.107.10**  Then, taking up another swift bow, that slayer of enemy heroes, enraged, pierced Bhagadatta in the battle with sharpened arrows.

**6.107.11**  That mighty archer, deeply wounded, licking the corners of his mouth again and again, hurled at Sātyaki a fearsome spear of iron, firm, adorned with gold and lapis, like unto the rod of Yama.

**6.107.12**  As it came hurtling suddenly, sped by the strength of that arm, Sātyaki in the battle, O king, cut it into three with his arrows; and it fell then to the earth like a great meteor with its luster quenched.

**6.107.13**  Seeing the spear struck down, your son, O lord of peoples, held Mādhava in check with a great host of chariots.

**6.107.14**  Seeing that great chariot-warrior of the Vṛṣṇis thus surrounded, Duryodhana, exceedingly pleased, spoke to all his brothers.

**6.107.15**  "Act so, O Kauravas, that Sātyaki shall not come forth alive from this great press of chariots; for once he is slain, I deem the mighty strength of the Pāṇḍavas slain."

**6.107.16**  Accepting his word with "So be it," those great chariot-warriors then fell to fighting the descendant of Śini before the eyes of Bhīṣma.

**6.107.17**  As Abhimanyu then came on, rising up against Bhīṣma in the fight, the mighty king of the Kāmbojas held him in check in the fray.

**6.107.18**  Arjuna's son, having pierced the king with smooth-jointed arrows, struck that king once again, O king, with sixty-four shafts.

**6.107.19**  But Sudakṣiṇa, in the battle, pierced the son of Kṛṣṇā's lord with five arrows and his charioteer with nine, wishing to preserve Bhīṣma's life.

**6.107.20**  Mighty was that combat there in their display of prowess, even as Śikhaṇḍin, scorcher of foes, rushed upon the son of Gaṅgā.

**6.107.21**  The aged Virāṭa and Drupada, those two great chariot-warriors, holding back the mighty host, in their fury rushed upon Bhīṣma in the battle.

**6.107.22**  Then Aśvatthāman, that best of chariot-warriors, came on enraged; and thereupon a battle broke out between your men and theirs, O descendant of Bharata.

**6.107.23**  Virāṭa, O scorcher of foes, struck with ten broad-headed shafts the son of Droṇa, that mighty archer who shone in battle as he strove.

**6.107.24**  And Drupada, drawing near, pierced the preceptor's son with three sharp arrows as he stood before Bhīṣma.

**6.107.25**  Then Aśvatthāman pierced both of them with ten arrows—the aged Virāṭa and Drupada, who had risen up against Bhīṣma.

**6.107.26**  There we beheld a marvel, the great feat of those two aged ones, in that they warded off the dreadful arrows of Droṇa's son in the fight.

**6.107.27**  As Sahadeva thus came on, Kṛpa, son of Śaradvat, advanced against him, as in the forest a rutting elephant charges another in rut.

**6.107.28**  And Kṛpa, in the battle, O king, swiftly struck the son of Mādrī, that great chariot-warrior, with seventy gold-decked arrows.

**6.107.29**  The son of Mādrī cut his bow in two with his arrows, and then pierced that bowless one with nine shafts.

**6.107.30**  Taking up another bow fit for the heavy work of battle, he, well pleased and enraged, struck the son of Mādrī upon the breast with ten sharpened arrows, wishing to preserve Bhīṣma's life.

**6.107.31**  In like manner the Pāṇḍava, O king, enraged, in his desire to slay Bhīṣma, struck the wrathful son of Śaradvat upon the breast; and a combat broke out between the two, dreadful of aspect and terrifying.

**6.107.32**  But Vikarṇa, scorcher of foes, pierced the enraged Nakula in battle with sixty arrows, guarding the life of Bhīṣma.

**6.107.33**  Nakula too, sorely wounded by your bow-wielding son, pierced Vikarṇa with seventy-seven sharp arrows.

**6.107.34**  There those two tigers among men, scorchers of foes, for Bhīṣma's sake, those two heroes smote each other like two bulls in a cattle-pen.

**6.107.35**  As Ghaṭotkaca strove in the battle, smiting your host, the valiant Durmukha advanced against him in the fray for Bhīṣma's sake.

**6.107.36**  Then the son of Hiḍimbā, O king, enraged, struck Durmukha, scorcher of foes, upon the breast with ninety sharpened arrows.

**6.107.37**  And Durmukha, that hero, exultant and roaring, pierced the son of Bhīmasena with sixty fair-pointed arrows at the head of the battle.

**6.107.38**  As Dhṛṣṭadyumna came on in the battle, eager to slay Bhīṣma, the son of Hṛdika held him in check, guarding the life of Bhīṣma.

**6.107.39**  The Vṛṣṇi hero, having pierced the brave son of Pṛṣata with five iron arrows, swiftly struck him again upon the breast with fifty more.

**6.107.40**  In like manner the son of Pṛṣata, O king, pierced the son of Hṛdika with nine keen, sharp arrows fledged with heron feathers.

**6.107.41**  A combat arose between the two in that great battle for Bhīṣma's sake, each striving to surpass the other, as once between Vṛtra and the great Indra.

**6.107.42**  As mighty Bhīmasena then came on toward Bhīṣma, Bhūriśravas swiftly advanced against him and cried, "Stand! Stand!"

**6.107.43**  Then the son of Somadatta struck Bhīma upon the breast in the fray with a very sharp iron shaft fletched with gold.

**6.107.44**  With that shaft lodged in his breast, the valiant Bhīmasena shone, O best of kings, as the Krauñca mountain of old with the spear of Skanda.

**6.107.45**  The two, enraged, cast at each other again and again in the battle arrows bright as the sun, polished by the smith.

**6.107.46**  Bhīma, eager to slay Bhīṣma, and the great chariot-warrior son of Somadatta, greedy for Bhīṣma's victory—each repaying blow with blow, the two fought on intently in the battle.

**6.107.47**  As Yudhiṣṭhira, O great king, surrounded by a mighty host, advanced straight toward Bhīṣma, the son of Bharadvāja held him in check.

**6.107.48**  Hearing the roar of Droṇa's chariot, like the thunder of the storm-cloud, the Prabhadrakas, O king, trembled, O honored one.

**6.107.49**  That great army of the son of Pāṇḍu, O king, checked in the fray by Droṇa, held firm and stirred not a step from its place.

**6.107.50**  As Cekitāna, enraged, advanced against Bhīṣma in the battle, O lord of men, your son Citrasena, terrible in his wrath, held him in check.

**6.107.51**  Putting forth his strength for Bhīṣma's sake, the great chariot-warrior Citrasena fought Cekitāna with utmost might, O descendant of Bharata.

**6.107.52**  Likewise Cekitāna too fought Citrasena; and mighty indeed was that combat there in their display of prowess.

**6.107.53**  But Arjuna, again and again checked by your son, turned that son of yours away and crushed your army.

**6.107.54**  Duḥśāsana too, with utmost might, held Pārtha in check, resolved that no enemy should slay Bhīṣma, O descendant of Bharata.

**6.107.55**  That host of your son, slaughtered in the battle, was churned up here and there by the foremost of chariot-warriors, O descendant of Bharata.

**6.108.1**  Saṃjaya said: Then the heroic mighty archer, with the prowess of a rutting elephant, taking up his great bow that could turn back a rutting elephant—

**6.108.2**  —that great chariot-warrior, brandishing his excellent bow, putting the great chariot-warriors to flight and laying low the army of the Pāṇḍavas—

**6.108.3**  —Droṇa, valiant and skilled in reading omens, surveying the portents on every side as he scorched the hostile ranks, spoke to his son:

**6.108.4**  "This is the day, dear son, on which the great chariot-warrior Pārtha, eager to slay Bhīṣma in the battle, will make his utmost effort.

**6.108.5**  For my arrows leap upward, my bow seems to quiver of itself, my weapons turn ready for use, and my mind dwells upon cruelty.

**6.108.6**  Though the quarters are still, beasts and birds cry out fearsomely, and the vultures settle low, facing the army of the Bhāratas.

**6.108.7**  The sun seems to have lost its splendor, the quarters are red on every side; the earth resounds and trembles, and the draught-beasts groan.

**6.108.8**  Herons, vultures, and cranes cry out again and again, and jackals with their inauspicious howls portend a great terror.

**6.108.9**  A great meteor has fallen through the midst of the sun's orb, and a headless trunk-shaped bar of darkness stands veiling the sun.

**6.108.10**  A dreadful halo too has formed about the moon and the sun, portending a fearful terror, the dismembering of the bodies of kings.

**6.108.11**  And the deities set in the shrines of the lord of the Kauravas tremble and laugh and dance and weep.

**6.108.12**  The planets have circled the moon, robber of its luster, from the inauspicious side, and the blessed moon has risen with its head turned downward.

**6.108.13**  I mark, too, that the bodies of the kings seem drained of luster, and in the armies of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son the mailed warriors shine not.

**6.108.14**  And throughout both armies on every side is heard the great blast of the Pāñcajanya and the twang of the Gāṇḍīva.

**6.108.15**  Surely, taking his stand and loosing his finest weapons in the fray, Bībhatsu, setting aside the other warriors in the battle, drives straight at the grandsire.

**6.108.16**  The pores of my skin bristle, and my mind seems to sink, mighty-armed one, as I ponder the meeting of Bhīṣma and Arjuna.

**6.108.17**  Setting before him that Pāñcāla of crooked cunning and evil mind, Pārtha has gone to the battle-ground of Bhīṣma.

**6.108.18**  And of old Bhīṣma declared: 'I will not slay Śikhaṇḍin, for by the Ordainer this one was fashioned a woman, and only by fate is he now a man.'

**6.108.19**  The great chariot-warrior, son of Yajñasena, bears an inauspicious standard; and the son of the River-goddess would not strike at one whose emblem is ill-omened.

**6.108.20**  As I ponder all this, my understanding sinks utterly; for this very day in the battle Pārtha has rushed upon the aged Kuru elder.

**6.108.21**  The wrath of Yudhiṣṭhira, the meeting of Bhīṣma and Arjuna, and the fury of my own weapons—surely all this bodes ill for the creatures of the world."

**6.108.22**  High-spirited, strong, and valiant, master of his weapons and firm in prowess, striking from afar with unerring shafts, and reading the omens well — such is the son of Pāṇḍu.

**6.108.23**  Unconquerable in battle even by the gods with Vāsava at their head, mighty and wise, having mastered all hardship, he is the foremost of warriors.

**6.108.24**  Ever victorious in war and wielder of terrible weapons is this son of Pāṇḍu. Avoiding his path, go swiftly, you whose vows are firm.

**6.108.25**  Behold, O mighty-armed one, the carnage that has drawn near — the great and splendid armor of heroes, wrought with gold,

**6.108.26**  these coats of mail are being shattered by arrows with their well-jointed shafts, and the tips of banners, the lances, and the bows are being cut to pieces,

**6.108.27**  and the bright keen spears, the gold-gleaming javelins, and the pennons of the elephants — all by the wrath of the Diademed One.

**6.108.28**  This is no time for those who live for their sons to guard their lives. Go forth, setting heaven before you, for glory and for victory.

**6.108.29**  This warrior whose banner bears the ape crosses in his chariot the river of battle — most dreadful and hard to ford, its whirlpools the steeds, the elephants, and the cars.

**6.108.30**  Devotion to the brāhmaṇas, self-restraint, generosity, and great austerity practiced — all this is seen here in the king whose brother is Dhanaṃjaya.

**6.108.31**  Mighty Bhīmasena, the two sons of Mādrī, and Vāsudeva of the Vṛṣṇis — these stand as his protectors.

**6.108.32**  Born of his wrath against the evil-minded son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, the anger of that king, his body seared by penance, now consumes the Bhāratas.

**6.108.33**  There Pārtha is seen, with Vāsudeva for his refuge, rending utterly all the armies of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son on every side.

**6.108.34**  Behold this host being thrown into turmoil by the Diademed One, like the vast mouth of a river bound up with great waves and churned by a leviathan.

**6.108.35**  Cries of woe and shrieks of dismay are heard at the front of the army. Go you against the heir of the Pāñcālas; I shall go against Yudhiṣṭhira.

**6.108.36**  Hard indeed to find is an opening to the king's battle-array of boundless splendor — like the belly of the ocean, hemmed in on every side by mighty car-warriors who stand their ground.

**6.108.37**  Sātyaki and Abhimanyu, Dhṛṣṭadyumna and Vṛkodara, and the twins guard the king, that lord of men, on every side.

**6.108.38**  Dark like Upendra, towering like a great sāla tree risen up, this one advances against the ranks like a second Phalguna.

**6.108.39**  Take up your finest weapons, and grasping another great bow, advance to the king's flank and do battle with Vṛkodara.

**6.108.40**  For who would not wish his beloved son to live through endless years? Yet setting the duty of a kṣatriya foremost, I therefore appoint you to this task.

**6.108.41**  And there too Bhīṣma in battle consumes the great host — in combat, dear son, the very peer of Yama and of Varuṇa.

**6.109.1**  Saṃjaya said: Bhagadatta, Kṛpa, Śalya, and Kṛtavarman of the Sātvatas, Vinda and Anuvinda of Avanti, and Jayadratha the lord of the Sindhus —

**6.109.2**  Citrasena and Vikarṇa, and the youthful Durmarṣaṇa — these ten warriors of your side gave battle to Bhīmasena.

**6.109.3**  Joined with a mighty host gathered from many lands, O king, they sought in the battle great renown for Bhīṣma's sake.

**6.109.4**  Śalya then struck Bhīmasena with nine arrows, Kṛtavarman with three, and Kṛpa with nine shafts.

**6.109.5**  Citrasena, Vikarṇa, and Bhagadatta, O venerable one, each struck Bhīmasena with ten broad-headed shafts.

**6.109.6**  And the lord of the Sindhus struck him with three arrows upon the collarbone; Vinda and Anuvinda of Avanti with five shafts each; and Durmarṣaṇa pierced the son of Pāṇḍu with twenty sharpened arrows.

**6.109.7**  Then, O great king, the mighty Bhīmasena pierced them all with many arrows — those resplendent heroes, the great car-warriors of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's party, renowned throughout the world — each one in turn.

**6.109.8**  Having pierced Śalya with fifty shafts and Kṛtavarman with eight, he cut through the middle of Kṛpa's bow together with its arrow, O Bhārata; and when his bow was broken, he pierced him again with five.

**6.109.9**  And he struck Vinda and Anuvinda with three arrows each, Durmarṣaṇa with twenty, and Citrasena with five.

**6.109.10**  Having pierced Vikarṇa with ten arrows and Jayadratha with five, Bhīma roared in delight, and struck the lord of the Sindhus once more with three.

**6.109.11**  Then, taking up another bow, the descendant of Gautama, foremost of car-warriors, pierced Bhīma in his fury with ten sharpened arrows.

**6.109.12**  Pierced by many arrows like a great elephant by goads, the mighty-armed and valorous Bhīmasena, enraged, then struck the descendant of Gautama with a host of shafts in the fray.

**6.109.13**  Then, with a splendor like that of Time the ender, he dispatched the steeds and the charioteer of the Sindhu lord to the realm of death with three arrows.

**6.109.14**  Swiftly leaping down from his car whose horses were slain, that great car-warrior hurled sharp arrows at Bhīmasena in the battle.

**6.109.15**  With two broad-headed shafts, O best of the Bhāratas, Bhīma cut through the middle of the bow of that high-souled lord of the Sindhus.

**6.109.16**  His bow severed, his car undone, his horses and charioteer slain, he climbed in haste, O king, onto the chariot of Citrasena.

**6.109.17**  A most wondrous deed the son of Pāṇḍu wrought there in the battle: that great car-warrior, having pierced and held off the mighty car-warriors with his arrows, stripped the Sindhu lord of his chariot before the eyes of all the world.

**6.109.18**  Śalya could ill brook the prowess of Bhīmasena. Fitting to his bow sharp arrows burnished by the smith, he pierced Bhīma with seventy of them, crying, "Stand! Stand!"

**6.109.19**  And Kṛpa, Kṛtavarman, and Bhagadatta, O venerable one, Vinda and Anuvinda of Avanti, and Citrasena in the battle,

**6.109.20**  Durmarṣaṇa and Vikarṇa, and the valiant king of the Sindhus — those tamers of foes swiftly pierced Bhīma for Śalya's sake.

**6.109.21**  But he pierced them in return with five arrows each, and pierced Śalya with seventy, and again with ten shafts.

**6.109.22**  Śalya, having pierced him with nine, pierced him again with five, and struck his charioteer deep in a vital part with a broad-headed shaft.

**6.109.23**  Seeing Viśoka pierced through, the valorous Bhīmasena planted three arrows in the arms and breast of the king of the Madras.

**6.109.24**  And the other great archers he struck, each with three straight-flying shafts, in the battle, and roared like a lion.

**6.109.25**  Those great archers, striving hard, struck the son of Pāṇḍu, intoxicated with battle, fiercely in his vital parts, each with three keen-pointed shafts.

**6.109.26**  Yet though sorely pierced, the great archer Bhīmasena did not waver — like a mountain under the streaming torrents of raining clouds.

**6.109.27**  And having fiercely pierced Śalya with nine arrows, the greatly renowned one pierced the lord of Prāgjyotiṣa firmly in battle with a hundred, O king.

**6.109.28**  Then with a very sharp razor-edged shaft he cut, with the skill of a deft hand, the bow of the high-souled Sātvata together with its arrow.

**6.109.29**  Then, taking up another bow, Kṛtavarman struck Vṛkodara between the brows with an iron shaft, O scorcher of foes.

**6.109.30**  But Bhīma in the battle, having pierced Śalya with nine iron shafts, Bhagadatta with three and Kṛtavarman with eight,

**6.109.31**  pierced the car-warriors led by Gautama with two arrows each; and they in turn, O king, pierced him in the battle with sharpened shafts.

**6.109.32**  Though thus afflicted on every side by those great car-warriors, he, accounting them no more than blades of grass, ranged about free of distress.

**6.109.33**  And those foremost of car-warriors, unflustered, sent forth at Bhīma their sharpened arrows by the hundreds and the thousands.

**6.109.34**  At him the heroic great car-warrior Bhagadatta hurled in the battle a javelin of great speed, gold-shafted and of great worth.

**6.109.35**  The Sindhu king cast a lance, and the mighty-armed one a barbed spear; Kṛpa hurled a śataghnī, O king, and Śalya an arrow in the fight.

**6.109.36**  Then the other great archers, aiming at Bhīmasena, sent forth five arrows each with all their might.

**6.109.37**  The son of the Wind cleft the lance in two with a razor-edged shaft, and cut the barbed spear with three arrows as if it were a sesame stalk.

**6.109.38**  And the mighty one shattered the śataghnī with nine heron-feathered shafts, and cut down the arrow loosed by the king of the Madras.

**6.109.39**  Swiftly he cut down the javelin hurled by Bhagadatta in the battle, and likewise the other dreadful shafts with his own well-jointed arrows.

**6.109.40**  Bhīmasena, glorying in war, clove each one of them into three, and struck all those great archers, each with three shafts.

**6.109.41**  Then, while that great battle raged, Dhanaṃjaya advanced there in his car, beholding the great car-warrior Bhīma — striking down the foes in the fray and contending with his arrows.

**6.109.42**  And beholding those two high-souled sons of Pāṇḍu joined together there, your men no longer hoped for victory, O bull among men.

**6.109.43**  Then Arjuna, contending in the battle with the great car-warrior Bhīṣma and longing for Bhīṣma's death, placed Śikhaṇḍin before him,

**6.109.44**  and fell upon those ten warriors of your side in the battle, O Bhārata, who stood there fighting against Bhīma, O king; and Bībhatsu pierced them, wishing to do Bhīma a kindness.

**6.109.45**  Then king Duryodhana urged on Suśarman for the slaying of both Arjuna and Bhīmasena.

**6.109.46**  "Suśarman, go swiftly, encompassed by your floods of troops, and slay these two sons of Pāṇḍu, Dhanaṃjaya and Vṛkodara."

**6.109.47**  Hearing that command of his, the Trigarta lord of Prasthala rushed in the battle upon Bhīma and Arjuna, those two bowmen,

**6.109.48**  and surrounded them on every side with chariots by the many thousands; thereupon the battle began between Arjuna and his foes.

**6.110.1**  Saṃjaya said: But Arjuna in the battle covered the striving great car-warrior Śalya with his well-jointed arrows.

**6.110.2**  He pierced Suśarman and Kṛpa with three arrows each, and the lord of Prāgjyotiṣa in the battle, and Jayadratha of the Sindhus,

**6.110.3**  and Citrasena and Vikarṇa and Kṛtavarman too, and Durmarṣaṇa, O lord of kings, and the two great car-warriors of Avanti —

**6.110.4**  each one he struck with three arrows fledged with the feathers of heron and peacock, that surpassing car-warrior, harrying your host in the fight.

**6.110.5**  Jayadratha in the battle, having pierced Pārtha with arrows, O Bhārata, swiftly pierced Bhīma, standing upon the chariot of Citrasena.

**6.110.6**  And Śalya in the battle, and Kṛpa the foremost of car-warriors, pierced the mighty-armed Jiṣṇu again and again with vital-piercing shafts.

**6.110.7**  And your sons led by Citrasena, O lord of peoples, swiftly in the battle struck Arjuna in the fray and Bhīmasena, O venerable one, each with five sharpened arrows.

**6.110.8**  Those two foremost of car-warriors, the sons of Kuntī, bulls among the Bhāratas, harried in the battle the great force of the Trigartas.

**6.110.9**  Suśarman too in the battle, having pierced Pārtha with many iron shafts, roared a mighty roar that made the very vault of heaven resound.

**6.110.10**  And other valiant car-warriors pierced Bhīmasena and Dhanaṃjaya with sharpened arrows, gold-nocked and straight-flying.

**6.110.11**  In the midst of those car-warriors the two sons of Kuntī, foremost of car-warriors, sporting in their splendid chariots and wondrous in form, shone resplendent — like two lions fierce in strength, greedy for prey, amid a herd of cattle.

**6.110.12**  Cutting asunder the bows of the heroes and their arrows in many ways in the battle, those two warriors struck down the heads of men by the hundreds.

**6.110.13**  Many chariots were shattered and horses slain by the hundred, and elephants together with their riders fell upon the earth in that great battle.

**6.110.14**  Charioteers and horsemen, struck down here and there, O king, were seen on every side writhing in their numbers.

**6.110.15**  With slaughtered hosts of elephants and foot-soldiers, with horses cut down, and with chariots broken in their multitudes, the earth was overspread.

**6.110.16**  With parasols hacked to pieces and banners cast down, with goads flung away and with horse-cloths, O Bhārata,

**6.110.17**  with armlets, bracelets, and necklaces, with trampled deerskin coverlets, with turbans flung away, and with yak-tail fans as well,

**6.110.18**  and here and there with the sandal-anointed arms and thighs of kings strewn about, the earth was overspread.

**6.110.19**  There we beheld the wondrous prowess of Pārtha in battle: checking those heroes with his arrows, he struck down your host.

**6.110.20**  But your son, beholding that onset of Bhīma and Arjuna, drew near to the chariot of the son of Gaṅgā in that great peril.

**6.110.21**  And Kṛpa and Kṛtavarman, and Jayadratha lord of the Sindhus, and the two Avantis Vinda and Anuvinda then came to the fray.

**6.110.22**  Then Bhīma the mighty archer and Phalguna the great chariot-warrior charged furiously against the dread host of the Kauravas in battle.

**6.110.23**  Then tens of thousands and millions of peacock-feathered arrows they swiftly let fall upon the chariot of Dhanaṃjaya in the encounter.

**6.110.24**  Then Pārtha, warding off those great chariot-warriors with a net of arrows, dispatched them on every side in battle to their death.

**6.110.25**  But Śalya, that great chariot-warrior, as though in sport, in his wrath struck Jiṣṇu upon the chest with smooth-jointed broad-headed shafts.

**6.110.26**  Pārtha, cutting through his bow and his leather guard with five arrows, then pierced him sorely in the vitals with keen shafts.

**6.110.27**  Then the lord of the Madras, taking up another bow able to bear the strain, enraged, smote Jiṣṇu in battle.

**6.110.28**  With three arrows, O great king, he struck Vāsudeva, with five, and Bhīmasena with nine, planting them in their arms and chests.

**6.110.29**  Then Droṇa, O great king, and the Māgadha, that great chariot-warrior, at Duryodhana's command, drew near to that place,

**6.110.30**  where Pārtha, O great king, and Bhīmasena the Pāṇḍava, those two great chariot-warriors, were slaying the mighty host of Kaurava.

**6.110.31**  But young Jayatsena in battle pierced Bhīma of dreadful weapons with eight whetted arrows, O bull of the Bharatas.

**6.110.32**  Bhīma, piercing him with ten arrows, struck him again with seven, and with a broad-headed shaft swept his charioteer from the chariot-seat.

**6.110.33**  Then by his frenzied horses, racing off in every direction, the Māgadha king was carried away while the whole army looked on.

**6.110.34**  But Droṇa, finding an opening, pierced Bhīmasena with sixty-five sharp-honed iron shafts.

**6.110.35**  Bhīma, glorious in battle, pierced that teacher, who was as a father to him, with nine broad-headed shafts and then with sixty, O Bhārata.

**6.110.36**  But Arjuna, piercing Suśarman with many iron shafts, scattered his army as the wind scatters great clouds.

**6.110.37**  Then Bhīṣma and the king, and the son of Subala, and Bṛhadbala, full of wrath, rushed upon Bhīmasena and Dhanaṃjaya.

**6.110.38**  So too the valiant Pāṇḍavas and Dhṛṣṭadyumna the son of Pṛṣata rushed in battle upon Bhīṣma, who was like Death with jaws agape.

**6.110.39**  And Śikhaṇḍin, drawing near to the grandsire of the Bharatas, charged him exultantly, casting off all fear, against that man of steadfast vows.

**6.110.40**  The sons of Pṛthā, led by Yudhiṣṭhira, placing Śikhaṇḍin at their head, fought Bhīṣma in battle, united together with the Sṛñjayas.

**6.110.41**  In like manner all your men, placing the man of steadfast vows at their head, fought the sons of Pṛthā led by Śikhaṇḍin in the encounter.

**6.110.42**  Then there arose a battle fearful to the Kauravas, between Bhīṣma and the sons of Pāṇḍu, contending for the victory.

**6.110.43**  In that battle Bhīṣma was the stake of your men, O lord of peoples; for there the game of dice had come round, for victory or for the contrary.

**6.110.44**  Dhṛṣṭadyumna, O great king, urged on all the troops: 'Charge upon the son of Gaṅgā! Fear not, O best of men!'

**6.110.45**  Hearing the words of their commander, the host of the Pāṇḍavas, casting aside their very lives in that great battle, swiftly advanced upon Bhīṣma alone.

**6.110.46**  And Bhīṣma, foremost of chariot-warriors, received that onrushing host, O great king, as the great ocean receives its shore.

**6.111.1**  Dhṛtarāṣṭra said: How, O Saṃjaya, did Bhīṣma the son of Śaṃtanu fight on the tenth day against the mighty Pāṇḍavas together with the Sṛñjayas?

**6.111.2**  And how did the Kurus in the fighting hold back the Pāṇḍavas? Tell me of the great battle of Bhīṣma, resplendent in war.

**6.111.3**  Saṃjaya said: How the Kurus fought together with the Pāṇḍavas, O Bhārata, and how that battle came to pass, I shall tell you as you listen.

**6.111.4**  Day after day the bands of chariots of your men, dispatched to the other world by the Diademed One with his supreme weapons, met their end.

**6.111.5**  And the Kaurava Bhīṣma, victorious in assemblies of war, true to his vow, wrought continual havoc among the hosts of the sons of Pṛthā.

**6.111.6**  Beholding Bhīṣma, that great chariot-warrior, fighting joined with the Kurus, and Arjuna with the Pāñcālas, men were filled with doubt as to the outcome.

**6.111.7**  On that tenth day, in that clash of Bhīṣma and Arjuna, there raged a most terrible and unceasing destruction of the hosts.

**6.111.8**  Therein, O king, Bhīṣma the son of Śaṃtanu, that scorcher of foes, knower of the supreme weapons, slew warriors by the ten thousand and yet more.

**6.111.9**  Those whose names and lineages were all but unknown, O king—all those heroes who turned not back were there slain by Bhīṣma.

**6.111.10**  Then, having scorched the Pāṇḍava host for ten days, Bhīṣma, righteous of soul, that scorcher of foes, grew weary of his life.

**6.111.11**  Swiftly seeking his own slaughter, face to face in battle, he thought: 'Let me not strike down these best of men who stand before me in the fray.'

**6.111.12**  So having reflected, the mighty-armed Devavrata, your father, O great king, spoke these words to the Pāṇḍava who stood near at hand:

**6.111.13**  'Yudhiṣṭhira, deeply wise, versed in all the scriptures, hear, my son, the word I speak, righteous and leading to heaven.'

**6.111.14**  'I am utterly weary, my son, of this body of mine, O Bhārata; and my time has been spent in slaying very many living beings in battle.'

**6.111.15**  'Therefore, placing Pārtha at the fore, together with the Pāñcālas and the Sṛñjayas, let effort be made for my slaying, if you would do me a kindness.'

**6.111.16**  Knowing that intent of his, the Pāṇḍava of truthful vision advanced intently against Bhīṣma in battle, together with the Sṛñjayas.

**6.111.17**  Then Dhṛṣṭadyumna, O king, and the Pāṇḍava Yudhiṣṭhira, having heard that speech of Bhīṣma, urged on their host.

**6.111.18**  'Charge! Fight! Conquer Bhīṣma in the encounter, guarded as you are by the truthful-vowed Jiṣṇu, victor over his foes!'

**6.111.19**  'And this mighty archer too, the son of Pṛṣata, commander of the host, and Bhīmasena in battle, will surely protect you.'

**6.111.20**  'No fear at all need be felt of Bhīṣma in the fight, O Sṛñjayas; surely we shall conquer Bhīṣma, placing Śikhaṇḍin at our head.'

**6.111.21**  Having thus made their compact, on the tenth day the Pāṇḍavas, intent on the world of Brahmā, came together swooning with wrath.

**6.111.22**  Placing Śikhaṇḍin and the Pāṇḍava Dhanaṃjaya at their head, they set themselves to the utmost effort for the felling of Bhīṣma.

**6.111.23**  Then, at the command of your son, the lords of many lands, mighty in strength, together with their armies, with Droṇa and his son,

**6.111.24**  and the powerful Duḥśāsana with all his brothers, then guarded Bhīṣma stationed in the midst of the battle.

**6.111.25**  Then your heroes, placing the man of steadfast vows at their head, fought the sons of Pṛthā led by Śikhaṇḍin in the encounter.

**6.111.26**  And he of the monkey-bannered chariot, joined with the Cedis and the Pāñcālas, advanced against Bhīṣma the son of Śaṃtanu, placing Śikhaṇḍin at the fore.

**6.111.27**  The grandson of Śini fought the son of Droṇa, Dhṛṣṭaketu the Paurava, and Yudhāmanyu fought Duryodhana together with his counsellors.

**6.111.28**  And Virāṭa, that scorcher of foes, with his army, closed with Jayadratha, heir of Vṛddhakṣatra, together with his host.

**6.111.29**  Yudhiṣṭhira, guarded by Bhīmasena, fell upon the mighty archer the king of the Madras with his army, and upon the elephant-division.

**6.111.30**  Against Droṇa—unassailable, irresistible, foremost of all bearers of weapons—the prince of Pāñcāla advanced intently together with the Somakas.

**6.111.31**  And the prince Bṛhadbala, the lion-bannered subduer of foes, advanced to meet the son of Subhadrā, whose banner bore the karṇikāra blossom.

**6.111.32**  And your sons, together with the kings, eager to slay, fell in battle upon Śikhaṇḍin and the Pāṇḍava Dhanaṃjaya.

**6.111.33**  In that most dreadful clash of valour between the two armies, as the divisions charged headlong, the earth quaked.

**6.111.34**  Those divisions, O Bhārata, clashed division against division, of your men and of the foe, at the sight of the son of Śaṃtanu in battle.

**6.111.35**  Then from those striving warriors rushing upon one another, a great clamour arose in all the quarters, O Bhārata.

**6.111.36**  With the blare of conches and kettledrums, with the trumpeting of elephants, and with the lions' roars of the troops, it grew terrible.

**6.111.37**  And the splendour of all those kings, like that of the moon and the sun, upon the armlets and diadems of the heroes, grew dimmed.

**6.111.38**  Clouds of dust arose, wreathed about with the lightning of weapons, and the twang of the bows grew terrible.

**6.111.39**  The whir of arrows, the blast of conches, the great din of drums, and the rumble of chariots all blended together in both the armies.

**6.111.40**  Thronged with masses of darts, spears, and lances, and with floods of arrows, the sky above the two armies grew as if without light.

**6.111.41**  Charioteers and horsemen fell upon one another in that great battle; elephants slew elephants, and foot-soldiers slew foot-soldiers.

**6.111.42**  That was a mighty battle of the Kurus with the Pāṇḍavas for Bhīṣma's sake, O tiger among men, as of two hawks contending for a piece of flesh.

**6.111.43**  Terrible was the encounter of those two hosts in the fight, O Bhārata, each bent on the slaughter of the other, eager for victory on the field of battle.

**6.112.1**  Saṃjaya said: Abhimanyu, O great king, valiant and joined with a great host, fought your son for Bhīṣma's sake.

**6.112.2**  Duryodhana, wrathful in battle, struck the son of Kṛṣṇā's sister with nine smooth-jointed shafts, and then again with three arrows.

**6.112.3**  At him the son of Kṛṣṇā's sister, in fury, hurled in battle a lance dreadful as Death's own sister, straight toward Duryodhana's chariot.

**6.112.4**  As that dread-shaped spear came hurtling suddenly upon him, O lord of peoples, your son, the great chariot-warrior, clove it in two with a razor-edged arrow.

**6.112.5**  Seeing the spear fall, the son of Kṛṣṇa, blazing with fury, planted three arrows in Duryodhana's arms and breast.

**6.112.6**  And again, O best of the Bharatas, he struck the unforgiving Duryodhana between the breasts with ten dreadful arrows.

**6.112.7**  That combat grew terrible and wondrous to behold, O Bhārata, a joy to the onlookers and honored by all the kings.

**6.112.8**  For the slaying of Bhīṣma and for Pārtha's victory the two heroes fought in battle — the son of Subhadrā and the bull among the Kurus.

**6.112.9**  The son of Droṇa, that bull among brāhmaṇas, scorcher of foes, in his wrath struck the impetuous Sātyaki upon the breast with an iron shaft.

**6.112.10**  The grandson of Śini too, O Bhārata, that warrior of measureless spirit, smote the teacher's son in all his vital parts with nine heron-feathered arrows.

**6.112.11**  Aśvatthāman in the battle struck Sātyaki with nine arrows, and then swiftly with thirty more in the arms and breast.

**6.112.12**  Deeply pierced by the son of Droṇa, that mighty archer of the Sātvata line, of great renown, struck the son of Droṇa with three arrows.

**6.112.13**  Paurava, closing in battle upon Dhṛṣṭaketu, that mighty archer and great chariot-warrior, tore him in many places with his arrows.

**6.112.14**  In the same way Dhṛṣṭaketu, the great chariot-warrior of vast strength, pierced Paurava in the fight with thirty sharpened arrows.

**6.112.15**  But Paurava, the great chariot-warrior, having cut Dhṛṣṭaketu's bow, roared a mighty roar and pierced him with ten arrows.

**6.112.16**  Then, taking up another bow, he struck Paurava with sharpened arrows, O great king, with seventy-three keen shafts.

**6.112.17**  There those two mighty archers, men of high station, great chariot-warriors, rained upon one another a vast downpour of arrows.

**6.112.18**  Having cut each other's bows and slain each other's horses, O Bhārata, those two great chariot-warriors, now bereft of cars, closed for combat with swords.

**6.112.19**  With bull-hide shields, bright and adorned with a hundred moons, and swords gleaming with great brilliance, dappled as with a hundred stars,

**6.112.20**  grasping their spotless blades, O king, the two rushed upon each other, eager as two lions in a great forest contending for a mate.

**6.112.21**  They wheeled in wondrous circlings, advancing and retreating, displaying their art and each seeking out the other's life.

**6.112.22**  Paurava, enraged, struck Dhṛṣṭaketu upon the temple with his great sword and cried, "Stand! Stand!"

**6.112.23**  The king of the Cedis too, in the battle, struck Paurava, that bull among men, upon the collarbone with the sharp edge of his great sword.

**6.112.24**  Those two subduers of foes, O great king, having closed with one another in that mighty fray, struck down by each other's onset, fell to the ground.

**6.112.25**  Then your son Jayatsena, lifting Paurava onto his own chariot, O king, bore him away from the field of battle.

**6.112.26**  And Dhṛṣṭaketu, in the fight, the son of Mādrī, the scorcher of foes, valiant Sahadeva, bore away from the battle, O king.

**6.112.27**  Citrasena, having pierced Suśarman with nine swift shafts, pierced him again with sixty, and yet again with nine arrows.

**6.112.28**  But Suśarman, enraged in the battle, O lord of peoples, pierced your son with ten and ten sharpened arrows.

**6.112.29**  And Citrasena, O king, in his wrath struck him in battle with thirty smooth-jointed arrows, and the other pierced him in return, O king, exalting in that fight the glory and honor of Bhīṣma.

**6.112.30**  The son of Subhadrā fought with the prince Bṛhadbala; and the lord of Kosala, having pierced the son of Arjuna with five iron shafts, pierced him again with twenty smooth-jointed arrows.

**6.112.31**  And the son of Subhadrā, having pierced Bṛhadbala with nine iron shafts, could not make him waver in the battle, yet pierced him again and again.

**6.112.32**  And the son of Phalguna once more cut the bow of the prince of Kosala and struck him with thirty heron-feathered arrows.

**6.112.33**  Then, taking up another bow, the prince Bṛhadbala, enraged, pierced the son of Phalguna in battle with many arrows.

**6.112.34**  A combat arose between those two for Bhīṣma's sake, O scorcher of foes — between the two enraged and wondrous fighters in that battle, O great king — even as once in the war of gods and demons between Maya and Vāsava.

**6.112.35**  Bhīmasena shone resplendent as he fought the host of elephants, like Śakra with thunderbolt in hand cleaving the loftiest mountains.

**6.112.36**  Those elephants, huge as hills, slaughtered by Bhīma, fell together to the earth, making the ground resound.

**6.112.37**  Those elephants, vast as mountains and dark as heaps of broken collyrium, lay shining upon the earth, scattered like sundered hills.

**6.112.38**  Yudhiṣṭhira, the mighty archer, closing in battle upon the king of the Madras, who was guarded by a great host, pressed him hard.

**6.112.39**  And the lord of the Madras, valiant for Bhīṣma's sake, enraged in the battle, pressed hard the son of Dharma, that great chariot-warrior.

**6.112.40**  The king of the Sindhus, having pierced Virāṭa with nine smooth-jointed, sharp shafts, tormented him again with thirty more.

**6.112.41**  And Virāṭa, O great king, at the head of the host struck the king of the Sindhus with thirty sharpened arrows between the breasts.

**6.112.42**  With bright bows and swords, with gleaming armor, weapons, and banners, those two of wondrous aspect — the Matsya and the Saindhava — shone resplendent in the battle.

**6.112.43**  Droṇa, meeting the son of the Pāñcāla in that great battle, made a vast tumult with his smooth-jointed arrows.

**6.112.44**  Then Droṇa, O great king, having cut the great bow of the grandson of Pṛṣata, pierced the grandson of Pṛṣata with fifty shafts.

**6.112.45**  Taking up another bow, the grandson of Pṛṣata, slayer of hostile heroes, loosed his shafts in the fight before Droṇa's very eyes.

**6.112.46**  Warding off those arrows with a multitude of his own, the great chariot-warrior Droṇa sent five shafts against the son of Drupada.

**6.112.47**  At this, enraged, O great king, the grandson of Pṛṣata, slayer of hostile heroes, hurled at Droṇa in battle a mace like the rod of Yama.

**6.112.48**  As that mace, adorned with bands of gold, came hurtling suddenly upon him, Droṇa warded it off in the fight with fifty arrows.

**6.112.49**  Severed in many pieces, O king, by the arrows loosed from Droṇa's bow, that mace, shattered and crumbling, fell to the surface of the earth.

**6.112.50**  Seeing his mace destroyed, the grandson of Pṛṣata, that slayer of foes, hurled at Droṇa a fair spear wholly wrought of steel.

**6.112.51**  That spear Droṇa clove with nine arrows in the fight, O Bhārata, and pressed hard in the combat the grandson of Pṛṣata, that mighty archer.

**6.112.52**  Thus did this great combat take place between Droṇa and the grandson of Pṛṣata, for Bhīṣma's sake, O great king — dreadful of aspect and terrifying.

**6.112.53**  Arjuna, reaching the son of Gaṅgā and pressing him with sharpened arrows, rushed upon him as he stood ready, like a hunter upon a rutting elephant in the forest.

**6.112.54**  And valiant Bhagadatta, of mighty strength, came forth against that Pārtha upon an elephant whose temples were split in three, blind with rut.

**6.112.55**  As that elephant, like the elephant of great Indra, came hurtling suddenly upon him, Bībhatsu, putting forth his utmost effort, met the charge.

**6.112.56**  Then king Bhagadatta the valiant, mounted upon his elephant, checked Arjuna in the fight with a downpour of arrows.

**6.112.57**  But Arjuna, in the great battle, pierced that oncoming elephant, white as silver, with spotless, sharp iron shafts.

**6.112.58**  And the son of Kuntī urged Śikhaṇḍin on, crying "Go! Go!" — "Toward Bhīṣma, O great king — slay him!" he said.

**6.112.59**  Then the lord of Prāgjyotiṣa, O elder of the Pāṇḍu line, leaving the son of Pāṇḍu, hastened, O king, toward the chariot of Drupada.

**6.112.60**  Then Arjuna, O great king, rushed swiftly upon Bhīṣma, placing Śikhaṇḍin before him, and thereupon the battle was joined.

**6.112.61**  Then those heroes of your side all rushed shouting upon the impetuous son of Pāṇḍu in the battle, and that was a marvelous sight.

**6.112.62**  The manifold ranks of your sons, O ruler of men, Arjuna scattered in due season, as the wind scatters the clouds in the sky.

**6.112.63**  But Śikhaṇḍin, closing upon the grandsire of the Bharatas, unflustered, swiftly covered him over with many arrows.

**6.112.64**  And Bhīṣma in the battle slew the Somakas who followed in Pārtha's train, and the great chariot-warrior held back the army of the Pāṇḍavas.

**6.112.65**  His chariot a fire-altar, his bow its flame, his swords and spears and maces its fuel, and his showers of arrows its great blaze, Bhīṣma consumed the warriors in the battle.

**6.112.66**  As a vast fire, wind-fanned, ranges through the dry brushwood, so too did Bhīṣma blaze, launching his celestial weapons.

**6.112.67**  With gold-feathered arrows, sharpened and smooth-jointed, Bhīṣma of great renown made the quarters and the intermediate quarters resound.

**6.112.68**  Striking down the chariot-warriors, O king, and the elephants together with their riders, he made the throngs of chariots like groves of headless palm-trees.

**6.112.69**  Bhīṣma, the foremost of all who bear arms, made the chariots, the elephants, and the horses void of men in that fight, O king.

**6.112.70**  Hearing the twang of his bowstring and the slap of his palm-guard, like the crashing of a thunderbolt, the soldiers trembled on every side, O king.

**6.112.71**  Unerring fell the arrows of your father, O lord of men; the shafts loosed from Bhīṣma's bow did not lodge but passed clean through the bodies.

**6.112.72**  We beheld chariots void of men, O king, well-yoked with swift horses, carried off like the wind, O lord of peoples.

**6.112.73**  Fourteen thousand warriors of the Cedis, Kāśis, and Karūṣas — renowned great chariot-warriors, men of noble birth, ready to lay down their lives —

**6.112.74**  those heroes who never turned back, with banners wrought of gold, on closing with Bhīṣma in battle, together with their horses, chariots, and elephants, went to the other world, as though to Death with gaping mouth.

**6.112.75**  There was not there, O great king, a single great chariot-warrior of the Somakas who, having reached Bhīṣma in the battle, kept his mind set upon living.

**6.112.76**  Beholding the prowess of Bhīṣma, men deemed all those warriors in the battle led away to the city of the king of the dead.

**6.112.77**  No great chariot-warrior went forth against him in the battle, save the heroic son of Pāṇḍu, he of the white steeds and Kṛṣṇa for charioteer, and Śikhaṇḍin the Pāñcāla of measureless might.

**6.112.78**  But Śikhaṇḍin, closing upon Bhīṣma in the battle, O bull of the Bharatas, struck him in that great fray with arrows by tens and tens.

**6.112.79**  But the son of Gaṅgā looked upon Śikhaṇḍin with an eye blazing with wrath, with a sidelong glance, as though to burn him up, O Bhārata.

**6.112.80**  Remembering his womanhood, O king, in the sight of all the world, Bhīṣma did not strike him in the battle; and Śikhaṇḍin did not perceive this.

**6.112.81**  But Arjuna, O great king, spoke to Śikhaṇḍin: "Make haste, hasten on, and slay this grandsire!

**6.112.82**  What need of speech, O hero? Slay Bhīṣma, that great chariot-warrior. For I see no other in all Yudhiṣṭhira's host

**6.112.83**  who is able to do battle in the field with Bhīṣma the grandsire, save you, O tiger among men — this truth I tell you."

**6.112.84**  Thus addressed by Pārtha, O bull of the Bharatas, Śikhaṇḍin swiftly rushed upon the grandsire with arrows of every kind.

**6.112.85**  Heedless of those shafts, your father Devavrata held off the wrathful Arjuna in battle with his own arrows.

**6.112.86**  And so too, O honored one, that great chariot-warrior dispatched the whole host of the Pāṇḍavas to the other world with his keen shafts in battle.

**6.112.87**  Likewise, O king, the Pāṇḍavas, surrounded by their great army, veiled Bhīṣma as clouds veil the day-maker sun.

**6.112.88**  Hemmed in on every side, that scion of Bharata, O bull of the Bharatas, burned down the heroes in battle as a blazing fire consumes a forest.

**6.112.89**  There we beheld the wondrous prowess of your son, who fought against Pārtha and guarded the firm-vowed one.

**6.112.90**  By that deed of your bowman son Duḥśāsana in battle, all the worlds were well pleased with the great-souled one.

**6.112.91**  For alone he fought against the Pārthas and their followers, and even so the Pāṇḍavas could not check that fierce warrior in the fray.

**6.112.92**  By Duḥśāsana in battle chariot-warriors were stripped of their cars, O great king, as were riders and mighty elephant-borne men.

**6.112.93**  Pierced through by his sharp shafts, they fell to the earth's surface, while other elephants, tormented by arrows, fled in all directions.

**6.112.94**  As fire, finding fuel, blazes up with fierce and brilliant flame, so did your son blaze, consuming the Pāṇḍavas.

**6.112.95**  That great captain of the Bharatas no mighty car-warrior of the Pāṇḍavas dared to vanquish, nor in any way to advance against him — save the son of great Indra, he of the white horses, whose charioteer was Kṛṣṇa.

**6.112.96**  For having conquered him in battle, O king, the victorious Arjuna rushed straight at Bhīṣma in the sight of all the army.

**6.112.97**  Though vanquished, your son, taking refuge in the strength of Bhīṣma's arm, again and again rallied and fought on, fierce in the battle; and warring against Arjuna in the fray, O king, he shone resplendent.

**6.112.98**  But Śikhaṇḍin in battle, O king, kept piercing the grandsire with arrows whose touch was as the thunderbolt's and like the venom of serpents.

**6.112.99**  Yet these wrought no pain in your father, O lord of men, and the son of Gaṅgā, smiling, then received those shafts.

**6.112.100**  As a man scorched by heat welcomes the streaming rain, so the son of Gaṅgā received the showers of Śikhaṇḍin's arrows.

**6.112.101**  The warriors beheld him, O great king, terrible in the fray — Bhīṣma, burning down the armies of the great-souled Pāṇḍavas.

**6.112.102**  Then your son said to all the troops, O honored one: 'Rush upon Phalguna in the battle with your chariots from every side!'

**6.112.103**  'Bhīṣma, knower of the law, will guard you all in battle. Casting off your great fear, fight back against the Pāṇḍavas!'

**6.112.104**  'Yonder Bhīṣma stands with his blazing palmyra banner, protecting all the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra — in battle their shelter and their shield.'

**6.112.105**  'Even the thirty gods, fully arrayed, are not able to assail the great-souled Bhīṣma — how much less the Pārthas, who are but mortals and feeble! Therefore charge, O warriors, and close with Phalguna in the fight!'

**6.112.106**  'I myself, intent in battle today, will fight against Phalguna, joined on all sides by your eager selves, O lords of the earth!'

**6.112.107**  Hearing that speech, O king, of your bowman son, the mighty great chariot-warriors made ready against Arjuna.

**6.112.108**  The Videhas and the Kaliṅgas, together with the bands of the Dāśerakas, and the Niṣādas and the Sauvīras, fell upon him in that great battle.

**6.112.109**  The Bāhlikas and the Daradas, the easterners and northerners, the Mālavas, the Abhīṣāhas, the Śūrasenas, the Śibis, and the Vasātis.

**6.112.110**  The Śālvas and their dependents, the Trigartas, the Ambaṣṭhas, together with the Kekayas — all rushed upon Pārtha in battle as moths upon a flame.

**6.112.111**  Calling to mind his divine weapons and fitting them to his bow, Dhanaṃjaya faced all those mighty car-warriors with their hosts, O great king.

**6.112.112**  With those weapons of mighty speed the great-strong Bībhatsu swiftly burned them up by the searing power of his shafts, as fire burns moths.

**6.112.113**  As that firm-bowed one loosed his thousands of arrows, the Gāṇḍīva was seen blazing as it were in the sky.

**6.112.114**  Tormented by his arrows, O great king, their chariot-banners scattered, the kings together could not stand against him of the ape-bannered car.

**6.112.115**  Car-warriors with their banners fell, horsemen along with their horses, and elephants with their riders, struck by the shafts of the Diademed One.

**6.112.116**  Then the earth lay covered with the arrows loosed from Arjuna's arms, and on every side by the routed forces of the kings fleeing many ways.

**6.112.117**  Then the mighty-armed Pārtha, having put the host to flight, sent forth his shafts in battle against Duḥśāsana.

**6.112.118**  Those iron-tipped shafts, piercing through your son Duḥśāsana, all entered the earth as serpents enter an anthill; and then he slew his horses and struck down his charioteer.

**6.112.119**  And with twenty shafts, O lord, he stripped Viviṃśati of his chariot, and smote him sorely with five smooth-jointed arrows.

**6.112.120**  Piercing Kṛpa, Śalya, and Vikarṇa with many iron shafts, the son of Kuntī, he of the white steeds, deprived them too of their chariots.

**6.112.121**  Thus those five — Kṛpa and Śalya, O honored one, Duḥśāsana and Vikarṇa, and likewise Viviṃśati — stripped of their chariots and vanquished by Savyasācin, fled away from the battle.

**6.112.122**  In the forenoon, O king, having thus defeated those great car-warriors, Pārtha blazed up in the battle like a smokeless fire.

**6.112.123**  And so too with his rain of arrows, like the radiant sun with his beams, he brought down still other kings, O great king.

**6.112.124**  Having turned the great car-warriors to flight with his showers of arrows, he set flowing in the battle a mighty river whose waters were blood, midway between the armies of the Kurus and the Pāṇḍavas, O Bhārata.

**6.112.125**  Elephants and bands of chariots were in many ways slain by the car-warriors; chariots were destroyed by elephants, and elephants by horsemen and foot-soldiers.

**6.112.126**  The bodies and heads of the warriors mounted on elephants, horses, and chariots, cut asunder in the midst, fell down in all directions.

**6.112.127**  The field of battle shone, strewn with princes, great car-warriors wearing earrings and armlets, the fallen and the falling alike.

**6.112.128**  Foot-soldiers were seen cut down by chariot-wheels and crushed by elephants, together with horses and the riders of horses.

**6.112.129**  Hosts of elephants, horses, and chariots fell on every side, and chariots lay shattered on the ground, their wheels, yokes, and banners broken.

**6.112.130**  Sprinkled with the blood of those streams of elephants, horses, and chariots, the covered battlefield shone like a red autumnal cloud.

**6.112.131**  Dogs, crows, and vultures, wolves together with jackals, and monstrous beasts and birds howled as they came upon their prey.

**6.112.132**  Winds of many kinds blew in all directions, while demons appeared to sight and spirits cried aloud.

**6.112.133**  Golden garlands and costly banners, swept up suddenly by the wind, were seen smoking as they whirled.

**6.112.134**  Thousands of white umbrellas and great chariots with their banners were seen scattered by hundreds and by thousands; and elephants with their pennons, tormented by arrows, fled in every direction.

**6.112.135**  And warriors, O lord of men, bearing maces, lances, and bows, were seen on every side fallen upon the earth's surface.

**6.112.136**  Then Bhīṣma, O great king, loosing a celestial weapon, charged upon the son of Kuntī before the eyes of all the bowmen.

**6.112.137**  Śikhaṇḍin, armored, rushed against him as he stood intent in battle; thereupon Bhīṣma withdrew that weapon, which was like fire itself.

**6.112.138**  At that very moment the son of Kuntī, he of the white steeds, having bewildered the grandsire, struck down your army.

**6.113.1**  Saṃjaya said: When the armies were thus arrayed and for the most part still standing fast, all the warriors became bent upon the world of Brahmā, O Bhārata.

**6.113.2**  For in that press army no longer clashed with army, nor chariots with chariot-warriors, nor foot-soldiers with foot-soldiers.

**6.113.3**  Horses fought not with horses, nor elephants with elephant-warriors; a great and terrible confusion arose between the two armies.

**6.113.4**  As men, elephants, and chariots were thus scattered everywhere, in that vast and dreadful carnage all distinction vanished.

**6.113.5**  Then Śalya and Kṛpa, and Citrasena, O Bhārata, and Duḥśāsana and Vikarṇa, those heroes mounting their chariots in haste, made the host of the Pāṇḍavas tremble in battle.

**6.113.6**  That army of the Pāṇḍavas, slaughtered in battle by those great-souled ones, found no rescuer, like a ship foundering in the water.

**6.113.7**  As the winter season cuts to the vitals of cattle, so did Bhīṣma cut into the vital parts of the sons of Pāṇḍu.

**6.113.8**  By your army and by the great-souled Pārtha alike, elephants resembling mountains and clouds were brought down in great numbers.

**6.113.9**  Leaders of men were seen being crushed by Pārtha, struck by his arrows and by his iron shafts in their thousands.

**6.113.10**  Here and there great elephants fell with a cry of anguish; and with the ornament-decked bodies of the slain great-souled ones,

**6.113.11**  and with heads still bearing earrings, the battlefield shone strewn over, in that exceedingly fearful slaughter of the bravest of heroes, O king, while Bhīṣma showed his valor in the fight and so did the Pāṇḍava Dhanaṃjaya.

**6.113.12**  Beholding the grandsire surging forward in the battle, O king, the Kauravas did not turn back, having set the world of Brahmā before them.

**6.113.13**  Desiring death in battle and making heaven their goal, they advanced against the Pāṇḍavas in that slaughter of the noblest heroes.

**6.113.14**  The Pāṇḍavas too, O great king, remembering the many and varied wrongs once done to them by you and your sons, O lord of men,

**6.113.15**  those heroes, casting off fear in battle and setting the world of Brahmā before them, fought joyfully against your men and your sons.

**6.113.16**  Then the commander, that great car-warrior, said to the army in the battle: 'Charge upon the son of Gaṅgā, O Somakas, together with the Sṛñjayas!'

**6.113.17**  Hearing the commander's word, the Somakas with the Sṛñjayas rushed upon the son of Gaṅgā with a rain of weapons from every side.

**6.113.18**  Being assailed, O king, your father, the son of Śaṃtanu, falling under the sway of wrath, fought against the Sṛñjayas.

**6.113.19**  To that illustrious one, O dear son, the wise Rāma had of old imparted the training in arms that destroys the armies of foes.

**6.113.20**  Relying on that training and wreaking destruction on the enemy's host, day after day the aged grandsire of the Kurus, Bhīṣma, slayer of hostile heroes, slew ten thousand of the Pārthas.

**6.113.21**  But when that tenth day had come, O bull of the Bharatas, by Bhīṣma alone, among the Matsyas and the Pāñcālas in the fray, after slaying countless elephants and horses, seven great car-warriors were slain.

**6.113.22**  The great-grandsire slew five thousand car-warriors, and fourteen thousand foot-soldiers in that great battle,

**6.113.23**  and a thousand elephants likewise, and again ten thousand horses, were slain by the might of your father's training, O lord of peoples.

**6.113.24**  Then, having thrown into turmoil the host of all the kings, the beloved brother of Virāṭa, Śatānīka, was struck down.

**6.113.25**  And having slain Śatānīka in battle, the valiant Bhīṣma, O great king, struck down thousands of kings with his broad-headed shafts.

**6.113.26**  And whatever kings among the Pārthas advanced toward Dhanaṃjaya, once they came upon Bhīṣma they went to the abode of Yama.

**6.113.27**  Thus, having covered the ten directions on every side with nets of arrows, Bhīṣma broke through the army of the Pārthas and took his stand at the head of the host.

**6.113.28**  Having wrought that mighty deed on the tenth day, he stood between the two armies with his bow grasped firm.

**6.113.29**  And none of the kings, O king, were able to gaze upon him, even as in the summer one cannot look upon the sun blazing at midheaven.

**6.113.30**  As Śakra scorched the host of the Daityas in battle, so did Bhīṣma scorch the sons of Pāṇḍu, O Bhārata.

**6.113.31**  And beholding him so valorous, the slayer of Madhu, the son of Devakī, spoke with affection to Dhanaṃjaya:

**6.113.32**  "Yonder stands Bhīṣma, son of Śaṃtanu, between the two armies. Unless you slay him by force, victory shall not be yours.

**6.113.33**  Be intent and hold him fast where this host is being shattered; for none other, O lord, has the strength to endure the arrows of Bhīṣma."

**6.113.34**  Then in that instant, O king, he of the monkey-bannered standard, thus urged, hid Bhīṣma—with his standard, his chariot, and his horses—from sight beneath his arrows.

**6.113.35**  But he too, the bull among the foremost of the Kurus, with showers of arrows scattered again and again the arrow-showers loosed by the Pāṇḍavas.

**6.113.36**  By him the king of the Pāñcālas, and valiant Dhṛṣṭaketu, and the Pāṇḍava Bhīmasena, and Dhṛṣṭadyumna son of Pṛṣata,

**6.113.37**  and the twins, and Cekitāna, and the five Kekayas as well, and Sātyaki, O great king, and the son of Subhadrā, and Ghaṭotkaca—

**6.113.38**  the sons of Draupadī, and Śikhaṇḍin, and valiant Kuntibhoja, and Suśarman, and Virāṭa—those mighty warriors of the Pāṇḍava side—

**6.113.39**  these and many others, tormented by the shafts of Bhīṣma and sunk in an ocean of grief, were drawn forth and rescued by Phalguna.

**6.113.40**  Then Śikhaṇḍin, swiftly seizing his supreme weapon, rushed straight at Bhīṣma, guarded by the diademed Arjuna.

**6.113.41**  Then the unvanquished Bībhatsu, skilled in the orderings of battle, having slain all who followed in support, rushed straight at Bhīṣma.

**6.113.42**  Sātyaki, and Cekitāna, and Dhṛṣṭadyumna son of Pṛṣata, and Virāṭa, and Drupada, and the two sons of Mādrī, the Pāṇḍavas, charged at Bhīṣma in the fray, shielded by the steadfast bowman.

**6.113.43**  And Abhimanyu in the battle, and the five sons of Draupadī, their great weapons raised aloft, charged at Bhīṣma in the fray.

**6.113.44**  All those firm-bowed warriors, never fleeing in battles, well practiced with their shafts, pierced Bhīṣma in many places with their arrows.

**6.113.45**  Shaking off those masses of arrows loosed by the foremost of kings, the grandsire, undaunted in spirit, plunged into the host of the Pāṇḍavas, warding off their shafts as though in sport.

**6.113.46**  Smiling again and again and recalling the womanhood of the Pāñcāla prince, Bhīṣma did not aim at him; heedless of Śikhaṇḍin's arrows, the great warrior struck down seven chariots in Drupada's array.

**6.113.47**  Then in an instant a tumultuous clamor arose from the Matsyas, the Pāñcālas, and the Cedis as they rushed upon him, the one man.

**6.113.48**  With their throngs of fine horses and chariots, with elephants and footmen, they covered that one man—Bhīṣma, son of Gaṅgā, who was scorching his foes in battle—as clouds cover the sun.

**6.113.49**  Then, in that battle between him and them, a battle like that of gods and demons, the diademed Arjuna, putting Śikhaṇḍin to the fore, assailed Bhīṣma.

**6.114.1**  Saṃjaya said: Thus all those Pāṇḍavas, putting Śikhaṇḍin to the fore, encircled Bhīṣma on every side and pierced him in the battle.

**6.114.2**  With dreadful śataghnīs, with spiked maces and battle-axes, with hammers, clubs, and javelins, and with slings on every side,

**6.114.3**  with gold-feathered arrows, with lances, pikes, and darts, with iron shafts, with calf-tooth barbs, and with bhuśuṇḍis, O Bhārata, all the Sṛñjayas together struck Bhīṣma in the battle.

**6.114.4**  Then, his armor shattered and tormented by many, the son of Gaṅgā did not waver, though his vital parts were being pierced.

**6.114.5**  With his blazing arrows and bow for flames, with the wind of his outpouring missiles, with the roar of his chariot-rims for thunder, and the rising of his great weapons for fire—

**6.114.6**  his wondrous bow a mighty blaze, the destruction of heroes its great fuel, Bhīṣma became to his foes like the fire at the end of an age.

**6.114.7**  Plunging in and bursting out through the masses of chariots, he was seen again, ranging in the very midst of the kings.

**6.114.8**  Then, passing beyond the king of the Pāñcālas and Dhṛṣṭaketu, he swiftly reached the center of the Pāṇḍava host.

**6.114.9**  Then Sātyaki and Bhīma, and the Pāṇḍava Dhanaṃjaya, and Drupada and Virāṭa, and Dhṛṣṭadyumna son of Pṛṣata—

**6.114.10**  these six he assailed with six arrows, terrible-sounding and swift, piercers of the foe's elephants, bright as the sun.

**6.114.11**  But those great warriors, warding off his sharp arrows, tormented Bhīṣma with might, each with ten shafts.

**6.114.12**  And the arrows that Śikhaṇḍin loosed in the battle, O you of great vows—gold-feathered and whetted on stone—swiftly entered Bhīṣma.

**6.114.13**  Then the diademed Arjuna, enraged, advanced straight upon Bhīṣma, and putting Śikhaṇḍin to the fore, cut his bow asunder.

**6.114.14**  The great warriors did not brook the cutting of Bhīṣma's bow—Droṇa, and Kṛtavarman, and the lord of the Sindhus, Jayadratha,

**6.114.15**  Bhūriśravas, Śala, Śalya, and Bhagadatta as well: these seven, exceedingly wrathful, rushed upon the diademed one.

**6.114.16**  Displaying their supreme celestial weapons, those great warriors, fiercely enraged, fell upon him and overwhelmed the Pāṇḍavas.

**6.114.17**  The sound of them as they bore down upon Phalguna was heard like the roar of oceans surging up at the dissolution of an age.

**6.114.18**  "Slay! Drive on! Seize! Fight! Cut him down!"—such was the tumultuous cry that arose against the chariot of Phalguna.

**6.114.19**  Hearing that tumultuous cry, the great warriors of the Pāṇḍavas ran forward seeking to protect Phalguna, O bull of the Bharatas.

**6.114.20**  Sātyaki and Bhīmasena, and Dhṛṣṭadyumna son of Pṛṣata, and Virāṭa and Drupada both, and the rākṣasa Ghaṭotkaca,

**6.114.21**  and the wrathful Abhimanyu—these seven, swooning with rage, swiftly charged forward, bearing their gaily-painted bows.

**6.114.22**  Between them arose a battle tumultuous and hair-raising, O best of the Bharatas, like the conflict of the gods with the Dānavas.

**6.114.23**  But Śikhaṇḍin, foremost of charioteers, guarded by the diademed one, pierced Bhīṣma—his bow already cut—with ten arrows in the fray, and his charioteer with ten, and with one he severed his standard.

**6.114.24**  The son of Gaṅgā took up another, swifter bow; but that too Phalguna cut to pieces with three sharp broad-headed shafts.

**6.114.25**  Thus the Pāṇḍava, enraged—Savyasācin, the scorcher of foes—cut asunder each bow of Bhīṣma's again and again as he took it up.

**6.114.26**  His bow cut through, and burning with wrath, licking the corners of his mouth, in his fury he seized a spear that could rend even mountains, and in his fury hurled it at the chariot of Phalguna.

**6.114.27**  Seeing it bearing down, blazing like a thunderbolt, the joy of the Pāṇḍavas took up five sharp broad-headed arrows.

**6.114.28**  In his wrath, O best of the Bharatas, he cut that spear—hurled by the might of Bhīṣma's arm—into five pieces with his five shafts.

**6.114.29**  Cleft asunder by the wrathful diademed one, it fell like a lightning-flash broken loose and sundered from a bank of clouds.

**6.114.30**  Beholding that spear cut to pieces, Bhīṣma, filled with wrath—the hero, conqueror of hostile cities—reflected in his mind upon the battle:

**6.114.31**  "I am able with a single bow to slay all the Pāṇḍavas, if only the mighty Viṣvaksena were not their protector.

**6.114.32**  For two reasons I shall not fight against the Pāṇḍavas: because the sons of Pāṇḍu are not to be slain, and because of the womanhood of Śikhaṇḍin.

**6.114.33**  Long ago, when he wedded Kālī, my father, well pleased, granted me death at my own will and invulnerability in battle. Therefore I deem this death to have come for me as if at its appointed hour."

**6.114.34**  Knowing thus the resolve of Bhīṣma of boundless splendor, the seers and the Vasus, standing in the sky, spoke to Bhīṣma:

**6.114.35**  "What you have resolved, O hero, is to us a thing most dear. Do it, O mighty bowman; turn your mind away from battle."

**6.114.36**  At the ending of that speech, an auspicious wind arose, favorable and fragrant, laden with droplets of water.

**6.114.37**  And the drums of the gods resounded with a mighty noise, and a rain of flowers fell upon Bhīṣma, O king.

**6.114.38**  And none, O king, heard those who spoke together, save mighty-armed Bhīṣma and myself, through the power of the sage.

**6.114.39**  And a great agitation seized the gods, O lord of peoples, that Bhīṣma, beloved of all the worlds, was then about to fall from his chariot.

**6.114.40**  Having heard this speech of the hosts of gods, the great-souled Bhīṣma, son of Śaṃtanu, no longer advanced against Bībhatsu, though pierced by sharp arrows that cleft through every armor.

**6.114.41**  But Śikhaṇḍin, O great king, in his wrath struck the grandsire of the Bharatas in the chest with nine sharp arrows.

**6.114.42**  Smitten by him in the battle, Bhīṣma, the grandsire of the Kurus, did not tremble, O great king, as a mountain trembles not in an earthquake.

**6.114.43**  Then Bībhatsu, laughing, drew his bow Gāṇḍīva and pierced the son of Gaṅgā with twenty-five small arrows.

**6.114.44**  Then again Dhanaṃjaya, hastening and full of wrath, struck him with a hundred arrows in all his limbs, in all his vital parts.

**6.114.45**  Thus, though grievously struck by others as well in that great battle, those gold-feathered, stone-whetted shafts wrought him no pain.

**6.114.46**  Then the diademed Arjuna, enraged, advanced straight upon Bhīṣma, and putting Śikhaṇḍin to the fore, cut his bow asunder.

**6.114.47**  Then, having pierced him with ten arrows, he severed his standard with one, and made his charioteer reel with ten shafts.

**6.114.48**  The son of Gaṅgā took up another, stronger bow; but that too, in less than the blink of an eye, he cut in three with three sharp broad-headed shafts, even as it was taken up, again and again, in that great battle.

**6.114.49**  Thus he cut asunder very many of his bows in the fray; and thereafter Bhīṣma, son of Śaṃtanu, no longer advanced against Bībhatsu.

**6.114.50**  Then he tormented him with twenty-five small arrows; and that mighty bowman, sorely pierced, spoke to Duḥśāsana:

**6.114.51**  "Yonder Pārtha, the great warrior of the Pāṇḍavas, enraged in battle, presses upon me alone in the fray with arrows by the many thousands.

**6.114.52**  He cannot be conquered in battle even by the wielder of the thunderbolt; nor are the heroes among gods, Dānavas, and rākṣasas, all together, able to vanquish me—how much less feeble mortals?"

**6.114.53**  While the two were thus speaking together, Phalguna, putting Śikhaṇḍin to the fore, pierced Bhīṣma in the fray with sharp arrows.

**6.114.54**  Then, sorely pierced by the sharp arrows of the wielder of Gāṇḍīva, Bhīṣma, smiling, spoke once more to Duḥśāsana:
